- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.31.0)): A how-to, [One proxy, several hosts](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/blob/main/Documentation/configuration/one-proxy-several-hosts.md), with the migration from several instances.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.196.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.196.0)): `Samples/Orders`: an application whose four screens fill the `OrdersList`, `OrderPage`, `OrdersFulfilmentBoard` and `OrdersDeliveryCalendar` templates and whose quick-create fills `OrdersQuickCreateOrder`, with the navigation declared in the layout, five specifications and a README.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.196.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.196.0)): The `orders-sample` conformance vector (golden syntax) and its entry in the sample list.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.59.0)): `@cratis/scene.blueprint.orders`: the orders list, the order page (key facts, lines, shipments, activity), the fulfilment board, the delivery calendar (the foundation `calendarGrid`) and the new-order quick-create dialog, with navigation, thumbnails, the `orders-en` sample-data pack and the `Business / Orders` category.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.59.0)): Specifications for the definition of done of every template, the screen set, the application behaviour, every forced standard state, and the Chromium audit at desktop, tablet and phone widths in both themes with colour contrast.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.59.0)): Docs `blueprints/orders-blueprint.md`, the table of contents and the index; the Orders package joins the browser specifications of the JavaScript build.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.72.0)): Analyzer CHR0065 warns when a read model has a method that is plainly meant to fold an event into it but will never be discovered as a self-reducer. This catches a fold whose name is not `Reduce` (for example `Reducer` or `reduce`) and a fold that is not `static`. Previously such a read model never folded and nothing said why. The diagnostic names the reason and shows the expected `Reduce` signature, and also recognizes historical event generations and event types that inherit `[EventType]`. See [CHR0065](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/code-analysis/CHR0065.mdx). (#4962)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.125.0)): The Stage ships `PrimeReact` and `Cratis.Blueprint.Default` in the catalog a `ui profile` is resolved against, next to `core`, `scene.web` and `Cratis.Components`. A domain blueprint (`@cratis/scene.blueprint.crm`, `.ticketing`, `.approvals`, `.inventory`, `.hr`, `.project-tracker`, `.administration`) now only needs approving itself. The declarations are generated from the real package manifests (`shippedPackages.spec.ts` fails when `Rendering.Cratis/Shipped/ShippedPackages.json` is stale), and `core:*` component names resolve.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.125.0)): A live Stage and a generated React application load the screen and dialog templates of the approved blueprints, so `template blueprint <Package> <Template>` renders the blueprint's template. A live Stage maps `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default`, `@cratis/scene.engine` and `@cratis/scene.model` to its own instances, as it does for React.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.125.0)): `Verification/live-blueprints` runs the Stage image against all seven published blueprints at 4.53.1 (approved set made from the published manifest as written) and asserts in a browser that every screen the blueprint's templates build mounts with content, with no load diagnostics and no sample data served. It is a new step of the `live-custom-packages` CI job.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.195.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.195.0)): `Calendar` feature under `TimeOff` in `Samples/Hr`: a `TeamCalendarEvents` read model projected from `TimeOffRequestRegistered`, a screen on the `HrTeamCalendar` template, a specification, and a *Team calendar* entry in the sidebar.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.195.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.195.0)): `Timeline` feature in `Samples/Projects`: a `ProjectTimelineItems` read model projected from `ProjectRegistered`, a screen on the `ProjectTimeline` template, a specification, and a *Timeline* entry in the sidebar.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.195.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.195.0)): The golden syntax of both samples regenerated for the conformance vectors `hr-sample` and `projects-sample`; the sample READMEs describe the new features.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.57.0)): `RootTemplate(Template: TemplateReference, Options)`, `ProfileAppearance(ColorScheme?, Accent?)`, `ProfileDefaults(NewScreenTemplate?)` and the `ColorScheme` enum (`Light`, `Dark`, `System`), and the optional members `UiProfile.Root`, `.Appearance`, `.Defaults` - in C# (`Cratis.Scene.Model`) and mirrored in TypeScript (`@cratis/scene.model`). The root template reuses the existing `TemplateReference` (own or blueprint, id, blueprint, version pin).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.57.0)): `UiProfileJson` (C# `Cratis.Scene.Engine.Profiles`) and `parseUiProfile`/`serializeUiProfile` (TypeScript engine): lenient reading, one canonical writing, byte-identical in both languages.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.57.0)): Shared fixture corpus `ui-profile-fixtures.json` (own, blueprint + pin, options, legacy layout only, nothing new, appearance, defaults, everything, lenient enum case, non-ASCII, unknown members) run by both toolchains; `scene-model-shape.json` extended for the new types and enum.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.57.0)): Documentation: new `blueprints/root-template.md`, `layouts.md` links to it.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.194.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.194.0)): `Diagnostic.Arguments` carries the subjects of a diagnostic as named values: text, keyword, index or declaration path. Each code declares its arguments, with their kinds and meanings, in the diagnostic catalog and the published contract. The names, kinds and meanings are permanent. Sixteen codes carry arguments in this release, among them PLAY0524 (an incomplete specification step), which names the specification, the step role and position, the missing property and its type, and the example or case. MCP and the `screenplay` tool include the arguments in their JSON output. See [Diagnostics](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/diagnostics.md). (#804)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.124.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.124.0)): Stage now accepts Screenplay models compiled to executable model version 24 (Screenplay 4.188.0), including the text validation rules `contains`, `not contains`, `starts with` and `ends with`. Generated validators, the specification runner and the host check them exactly as Screenplay does: case-sensitive, and an absent value passes. (#448)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.56.0)): `ProjectTimeline` screen template and the `projects.timeline` screen in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.project-tracker`: every project as a bar in a lane for its owner with progress, two milestones and dependencies, built on the foundation `timelineGrid`; the scale is kept in the address and on a phone the schedule is the table.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.56.0)): Timeline items and columns, and the day the schedule counts from, in the `project-tracker-en` sample-data pack.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.56.0)): A `Timeline` entry in the navigation, a thumbnail, and the docs in `blueprints/project-tracker-blueprint.md` (the not-in-delivery note is removed).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.55.0)): `HrTeamCalendar` screen template and the `hr.timeoff.calendar` screen in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.hr`: who is away and when on a month, a week or as an agenda, built on the foundation `calendarGrid`; a filter by person or kind of leave; the people away listed beside it; one column on a phone.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.55.0)): Calendar events, columns and filter fields in the `hr-nordic` sample-data pack, made from its time-off requests (a declined request is left out).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.55.0)): A `Team calendar` entry beneath Time off in the navigation, a thumbnail, and the docs in `blueprints/hr-blueprint.md` (the not-in-delivery note is removed).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.54.0)): Components in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default`: `calendarGrid` (month, week, agenda; keyboard; the busy day listed in full; day and view in the address), `timelineGrid` (Gantt-style lanes with progress, milestones, dependencies and conflicts told in words; a table alternative) and `inboxList` (grouped by day, unread told in words, filter and category in the address, keyboard, mark read or unread).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.54.0)): Foundation screen templates: `CalendarScreen`, `TimelineScreen`, `InboxScreen`, `SettingsScreen`, `ProfileScreen`, `ReportViewer` and `ImportWizard`, each with a thumbnail and preview data from the foundation sample-data pack.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.54.0)): The template types `Calendar`, `Timeline`, `Inbox`, `Settings`, `Profile`, `Report` and `Import` in the type vocabulary of both the TypeScript and the C# engine, with the shared verification fixture.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.54.0)): Specifications: behaviour of each component, definition of done and preview of each template, and the Chromium audit of every new component at desktop, tablet and phone widths in both themes.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.54.0)): Docs: `blueprints/foundation-components.md` and `blueprints/foundation-templates.md`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.193.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.193.0)): Application-root `command reads protected` preamble opts a model into protected command reads, bound as ESM v25. Declare it once in the root document; imported commands inherit it. See [Commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/commands.md) and [decision 0085](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0085-admit-protected-command-reads-through-an-explicit-preamble.md). (#129)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.193.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.193.0)): Under the preamble, `read.property` paths qualify single-level read-model values in `require` conditions and in produced-event mappings. (#802)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.193.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.193.0)): The reference evaluator rejects a command with `Validation` when a required read-model instance is absent, and with `Conflict` (`DecisionStateChanged`) when a matching fact is appended between decision and commit. (#129)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.193.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.193.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY0780` to `PLAY0785` report a misplaced or duplicate preamble, a read that cannot be protected (with its closed reason), a read without a key, reads combined with `concurrency`, and unknown or ambiguous read paths. (#129)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.193.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.193.0)): Informational `PLAY0786` on eligible legacy models, with a typed workspace and MCP repair that previews inserting the preamble for individual review before apply. (#129)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.193.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.193.0)): Syntax, highlighting, completion and diagnostics for the preamble in the TypeScript compiler, the Monaco editor and the VS Code extension. (#129)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.192.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.192.0)): A runnable application shell vector. `ScreenCompositionCorpus.V1` publishes `screen-shell-navigation-runnable`: the shell constructs (a topbar and sidebar that inherit, extend and override per module, item targets and slots, a toolbar button that starts a State Change slice, a sample data pack the ui profile selects) with the queries, projections and screens the generated backend needs. The structure-only `screen-shell-navigation-positive` case is not a backend model - `cratis render` answers `PLAY0268` and `PLAY0269` for it - so the CLI leg reads this case; both stay in the corpus.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.192.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.192.0)): `BrowserHarness/screen-shell-cli-render.sh`, also a `shell-cli` step of `run-public-acceptance.sh`: it renders the case, from the published corpus package when `SCREENPLAY_CORPUS_PACKAGE_VERSION` is set, with the installed public `cratis` (3.67.0 or later, which bundles Stage 4.119.1) and requires the module navigation targets and the toolbar slice in `scene.json` and no sample data anywhere in the generated application.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.122.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.122.0)): Stage now accepts Screenplay models compiled to executable model version 23 (Screenplay 4.182.0) wherever an equivalent earlier model was accepted. (part of #316)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): `GenerationStrategyCatalog` in `Cratis.Screenplay.Semantics` lists every generation strategy and what it accepts:
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): the ESM kind, primitives and the version that introduced it;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): whether it is admitted or reserved, and whether it needs an implementation;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): its fixed entropy;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): each option's placement, kind, limits, choices, default and conditions.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): `SemanticConceptCheck.Check` checks one concept, given as syntax or from an executable model, and returns:
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): its findings, each pointing at the generation, an option or a rule rather than a source location;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): its rule proofs;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): its generation support and entropy;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): the ESM version it requires.
- **cli** ([Release v3.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.68.0)): A spec evaluates every `plan.*` assertion the canonical corpus pins for its application shell vector (`screen-shell-navigation-positive`, Stage `Desktop` plan) - topbar and sidebar regions at application level and in three modules (inherit, extend, override), hierarchical items with target and slot, toolbar buttons that invoke State Change slices, blueprint template references, slice kinds, and no served sample data - against the Scene the CLI hands to its renderer for that document. A path the corpus adds is refused until the CLI understands it. (part of #367)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): `@cratis/scene.blueprint.approvals`, a blueprint package (domain `approvals`, category `Business / Approvals`): seven screen templates, two dialog templates and seven ready-to-use screens.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): `ApprovalInbox`: the requests waiting for the signed-in person as cards (the foundation list's Cards variant) with **Approve, Reject and Request changes as buttons on each card**; a decision opens `DecisionDialog`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): `MyRequests` (draft, submitted, approved, rejected as chips, and a New request button), `AllRequests` and `RequestTypes` (the request types an organisation offers, for administrators).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): `NewRequestWizard`: request type, details, attachments (a file uploader), then review and submit, on the foundation `WizardScreen`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): `RequestPage`: summary, the **approval chain** (`approvalChainStepper`), the request's fields, attachments and the comments as the page's activity, and the decision buttons.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): `RequestTypeEditForm`, and dialogs `DecisionDialog` (a confirmation with the decision and a comment) and `RequestTypeQuickCreate`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): Each template carries its `scopes`, so a template picker offers it only where it fits.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): Navigation: a topbar naming the `approvals` module and the module's sidebar (Inbox, My requests, All requests, Templates), the application structure and `approvalsApplication()`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): Sample-data packs `approvals-general` (en-GB, GBP) and `approvals-nordic` (nb-NO, NOK) answering the same shapes, with five request types as presets (Leave, Expense, Purchase, Travel, Invoice approval).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): Specifications: definition of done per template and package, screen set consistency, applicability by level, every screen previewed in both packs with no accessibility violation, standard states forced on seven screens, the behaviors of the application (decide from the inbox, start and step through the wizard, open a request and its chain, edit a request type), and a Chromium audit of every screen and dialog at desktop, tablet and phone widths in light and dark themes (56 specs, in CI).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): Docs: `blueprints/approvals-blueprint.md`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.190.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.190.0)): `Samples/Approvals/approvals.play`: requests and approvals - an inbox of decision cards, my requests, a new-request wizard, a request page with its approval chain, every request and an administrator's request types - in one file of 6 slices, each with a specification (6 of 6 pass under `screenplay test`). Every screen fills a template of the blueprint (`template blueprint Cratis.Blueprint.Approvals ApprovalInbox version "^1.0"`); the decision dialog and the wizard give their `actions` slot the command they issue; the layout declares the topbar and sidebar; the `ui profile` activates the blueprint's English pack with `active blueprint approvals-general`. It compiles with no error and no warning.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.190.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.190.0)): The conformance manifest lists the sample (`approvals-sample`) with its golden syntax, so the TypeScript and C# compilers are held to the same tree for it.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.190.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.190.0)): Docs: `Samples/Approvals/README.md`, the samples table and the samples rule.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.52.0)): `dataList` accepts `rowActionStyle` (`menu`, the default, or `buttons`). With `buttons` each row action is a button of its own in the row or card, named for its request ("Approve: Leave: Isla Morgan"), publishes the same `action` output as the menu, and follows the action permission rule (disabled buttons say why on hover, hidden ones are not drawn).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.52.0)): Docs: `blueprints/foundation-components.md` (Deciding from a list).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.189.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.189.0)): `Samples/Inventory` (warehouse: items, an item gallery, locations, movements, check in and out, suppliers, a dashboard), `Samples/Hr` (directory, org chart, time off with balances, onboarding), `Samples/Projects` (projects, my tasks, a board by status and by person, a task side sheet) and `Samples/Administration` (users, roles and permissions, settings, audit log). Each is one file with a persona and policy, the layout's topbar and sidebar navigation, a `ui profile` that activates the blueprint's pack (`active blueprint inventory-warehouse`, `hr-nordic`, `project-tracker-en`, `admin-default`) and Given/When/Then specifications for every slice (`screenplay test` passes). They compile with no error and no warning under `--warnaserror`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.189.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.189.0)): The conformance manifest lists the four samples with their golden syntax, so the TypeScript and C# compilers are held to the same tree for them (`SCREENPLAY_REGENERATE_SYNTAX_VECTORS=1`).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.189.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.189.0)): Docs: a README for each sample, the samples table and the samples rule.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): `@cratis/scene.blueprint.ticketing`, a blueprint package (domain `ticketing`, category `Business / Ticketing`): nine screen templates, six dialog templates and nine ready-to-use screens.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): `SupportDashboard`: open tickets, overdue and the average first response; open tickets by priority and by assignee; the overdue tickets; my next steps.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): `TicketQueue` (id, subject, requester, priority and status chips, assignee, age; saved views Unassigned, Mine and Overdue), `MyTickets` and `CustomerList`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): `TicketBoard`: a column for each status with a card for each ticket.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): `TicketPage`: the conversation as the page's activity, the properties in the aside, and the quick actions Assign, Change status, Escalate and Merge, each opening its dialog.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): `TicketCreateForm` and the public-facing `TicketPortalForm` (full-page layout, no application chrome, a notice about what happens next, only what a customer can say).

- **Scene** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): Dialogs `TicketQuickCreate`, `CustomerQuickCreate`, `AssignTicket`, `ChangeTicketStatus`, `EscalateTicket` and `MergeTickets`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): Navigation: a topbar naming the `support` module and the module's sidebar (Dashboard, Queue with List and Board, My tickets, Customers, Knowledge base), the application structure and `ticketingApplication()`.

- **Scene** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): Specifications: definition of done per template and package, screen set consistency, applicability by level, every screen previewed in both packs with no accessibility violation, standard states forced on seven screens, the behaviors of the application (quick create, open a ticket, the four quick actions, board moves, the portal form without chrome), and a Chromium audit of every screen and dialog at desktop, tablet and phone widths in light and dark themes (92 specs, in CI).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): Docs: `blueprints/ticketing-blueprint.md`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.188.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.188.0)): Canonical application shell vector. `ScreenCompositionCorpus.V1` publishes `screen-shell-navigation-positive`: an application with a sidebar and a topbar at application level and in three modules (inherit, extend, override), hierarchical items with target and slot, toolbar buttons that invoke State Change slices, blueprint template references and a sample data pack the ui profile selects. Seven rejection vectors pin the diagnostics of the shell rules (`PLAY0710`, `PLAY0714`, `PLAY0721`, `PLAY0722`, `PLAY0744`, `PLAY0747`-`PLAY0749`, `PLAY0761`, `PLAY0762`) five MCP edit expectations that name the transcripts editing each construct, and a `Desktop` Stage plan pinning what the published Stage image `cratis/stage:4.119.1` serves for the document (navigation, toolbar buttons, blueprint references; no sample data).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.188.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.188.0)): Revision-checked MCP transcripts for navigation regions, toolbar buttons and blueprint template references: read the typed nodes, propose, refuse a stale revision, apply, reopen. Switching a module's region mode, copying the application's items (retargeted to the module, `PLAY0750` otherwise), adding and removing sub-items, pointing a button at a State Change slice (`PLAY0762` for anything else) and changing a template version range are all ordinary `propose-ast` edits. See [Author the application shell](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/mcp/authoring-tools.md#author-the-application-shell).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.188.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.188.0)): The MCP `syntax-schema` describes every new kind (`NavigationRegionSyntax`, `NavigationItemSyntax`, `NavigationTargetSyntax`, `NavigationLogoSyntax`, `ToolbarButtonSyntax`, template references, sample data), asserted by the transcript spec.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.188.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.188.0)): The shared TypeScript conformance vectors hold the whole shell document and module and feature template assignments, so both compilers accept what the C# compiler accepts.

- **Scene** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): Docs: `blueprints/crm-blueprint.md`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.43.0)): `kanbanBoard` can total a numeric card field in each column header: name the field in `totalField` (a deal's `amount`) and `format` (`currency` with a `currency` code, `percent`, `compact` or a plain number); the totals are formatted in the element's `locale` and follow a card as it is moved, by dragging or by its Move-to menu.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.43.0)): Docs: `blueprints/foundation-components.md` (Boards).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.185.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.185.0)): `SemanticValidationRules.Check` in `Cratis.Screenplay.Semantics` checks a value against one rule, against every rule of a concept, or against a type, walking composites and collections. Each `SemanticRuleCheck` says whether the rule was satisfied, failed or could not be evaluated, and carries the rule, its message and, for `matches`, the pattern check. It is the same implementation the reference evaluator uses: absent values pass every rule except not-empty, text compares ordinally, numbers compare as exact decimals, and Studio text-rule patterns evaluate as their first-class rule. See [Check values against validation rules](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/value-rule-checks.md). (#811)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.184.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.184.0)): Concept and command rules `contains "x"`, `not contains "x"`, `starts with "x"` and `ends with "x"` on text values. They compare UTF-16 code units ordinally, an absent value passes, and they are written to the executable model in a new ESM version; models that do not use them keep their bytes and version. See [Commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/commands.md). (#810)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.184.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.184.0)): Public pattern admission checks `PortableMatchPattern.Check(pattern)` in .NET and `checkPortableMatchPattern(pattern)` in `@cratis/screenplay-compiler`. They report whether a `matches` pattern is admitted, deprecated, refused or invalid, with the diagnostic code, the construct and its offset, a suggestion, and the first-class replacement for a Studio pattern. (#810)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.119.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.119.0)): Concepts with an ESM v21 `generate pattern` (optionally with a checksum) or `generate random a..b` strategy now render: generated applications create values with Cratis.Fundamentals `GenerateValue.Pattern` and `GenerateValue.Integer`, dated patterns take their date from the generation context at run time, and Stage's executor and Host check pinned specification values against the pattern or range with the same semantics as Screenplay. (part of #414)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.107.0)): Screenplay generation recovers pattern and signed integer random strategies, checksum validation, and self-contained C# generation rules using `Now`, `Tenant`, and `GenerateValue` context overloads. (#3116)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.107.0)): Generated-value pins are checked against recovered generation contracts, while executable model version caps retain unsupported validation in source rather than silently dropping it.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.118.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.118.0)): Concepts with an ESM v21 `validate checksum` rule (Luhn, Mod11 or Mod97) now render: the generated concept validator checks the checksum with Cratis.Fundamentals, and Stage's executor and Host evaluate the rule with the same semantics as Screenplay. Pinned specification values that fail the checksum are refused when the model is admitted. (part of #414)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.182.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.182.0)): A command can declare a pending `handler implementation` intent with ordered `hint`s beside unconditional `produces`. The body owns only the event payload values. Which events, their order, destinations, tags, generated values, the response, authorization and validation stay declarative, and event-property mappings become non-normative hints, so required event properties may be left unmapped. See [Commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/commands.md). (#782)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.182.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.182.0)): The executable semantic model version 23 carries a described command's handler requirement (`handler { requirementId, resultVersion }`), and the requirements sidecar reports it as a Pending CommandHandler requirement with its hints and provisional/ambiguous flags. Models without a described command keep their previous version and bytes, and consumers that do not admit version 23 refuse the model rather than dropping the described logic. (#782)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.182.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.182.0)): Diagnostics PLAY0695 (a command is described but not implemented), PLAY0696 (a described command has no productions or only a response), PLAY0697 (conditional productions under a pending intent) and PLAY0698 (a pending intent next to another handler, in both compilers). (#782)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.182.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.182.0)): Reference execution reports a specification that reaches a described command's body as Unsupported (`ImplementationBody`), naming the command and its requirement, directly and through reactions. Authorization and validation outcomes are evaluated as before. (#782)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.182.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.182.0)): The MCP `handler-intents` and `implementation-requirements` views report pending requirements with their hints and flags, and whether each described command is admitted. (#782)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): Read protection modes `Off`, `Measure` and `On`, set with `[assembly: ReadProtection(...)]`, `[ReadProtection(...)]` on a command or the MSBuild property `<CratisReadProtection>`. `ReadProtection:MaximumMode` lowers the mode at runtime and logs a warning at startup (#3268)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): Under `On`, read-model parameters of `Handle()` and of a `CommandValidator<T>` constructor are read fresh from the event log and guard the command's append. If one changed before the commit, nothing from the command is written and the caller gets a 400 saying which read model changed and to submit again (#3268)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): `[Unprotected("why")]` marks a read-model parameter, type or method body as context that does not guard the write (#3268)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): `[LookUpBy(nameof(Command.Property))]` names the command property that is the key of a read-model parameter when it is not the event source id (#3268)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): Under `On`, reads that cannot be protected are refused with a message that says why and what to do instead, including raw collection reads, filtered or constructor-dependent reducers, immediate appends and shared validator instances (#3268)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): `Measure` leaves behaviour unchanged and reports which reads `On` would guard, refuse or find changed, through startup warnings, periodic log summaries and telemetry (#3268)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): Read models declared with `[EventStream<TSource>(stream)]` can be plain parameters of a command or validator: Arc reads them from the stream the command's route points to, so a monthly limit is guarded by that month only (#3306)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): Analyzers ARCCHR0024, ARCCHR0028 and ARCCHR0033 to ARCCHR0038 report reads that cannot be protected, aggregates and reads on the same source, unprotected method bodies, early aggregate commits, unproven exact scopes, read-model streams that are not on the command route and stream guards that widen (#3268) (#3306)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): A guide to keeping command decisions correct: choosing a mechanism, getting started, adopting with Measure, protected and advisory reads, validators, refusals, how guards are derived, and scenarios from a single entity to per-stream limits and processes (#3268)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.116.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.116.0)): `ArtifactImplementationGap`, `ArtifactImplementationStub`, `ArtifactImplementationContext` and `ArtifactImplementationBodyLocator` describe a gap, its requirement id, role, hints, and each typed stub with its file, signature, body location and acceptance specifications. (part of #316)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.116.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.116.0)): `ArtifactRenderPlan.ImplementationGaps`, forwarded through `CratisPlanResult`. A plan with gaps includes them in its digest; a plan without gaps keeps its existing digest. (part of #316)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.116.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.116.0)): `ImplementationGapMode` (`Refuse` or `Stub`) on `CratisPlanOptions` and `CratisRenderingOptions`. It defaults to `Refuse` and has no effect until a target supports stubs. (part of #316)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Dialog templates in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default`, category `Foundation / Dialog`: `QuickCreateDialog` (six fields at most, Cancel and Create), `LookupDialog` (search and a list, single or multiple selection), `SideSheet` (a drawer from the inline end hosting a form or record) and `FilterDialog` (the filter builder; fills the page on a phone). `ConfirmDialog` now lists the consequences, takes a `danger` tone and names its verb on the confirming button; `FormDialog` and `DetailDialog` gain the same frame.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Auth and state screens fitting the full-page layout, categories `Foundation / Auth` and `Foundation / State`: `LoginScreen` (logo, form, identity-provider slot, live-region error area, help link), `NotFoundScreen`, `ForbiddenScreen` (binds no data, so nothing leaks) and `ErrorScreen` (retry and the support reference id).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Foundation components: `dialogFrame` (heading, message, consequences, close button; gives the host's dialog element its accessible name, focus on open, a Tab trap, Escape, focus restored on close, placement `center`/`side`/`full`, a phone filling the page, RTL mirroring), `actionButton` (permission rule enabled/disabled/hidden, behaviors, danger emphasis) and `notice` (a live-region message); a `password` field type and a `saveAndNew` switch on the form.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Cancel and Close carry a `CloseDialog` action as data, so the same document closes the dialog in Scene, Stage and the preview.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): `GalleryDialogPreview`, stories for each dialog state, sample-data pack shapes for the dialogs and screens, thumbnails for every template.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): `verifyTemplateDefinitionOfDone` now also takes a dialog template.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Docs: `blueprints/foundation-templates.md` (dialogs, auth and state screens, aliases), `foundation-components.md`, `template-set.md`, `pattern-coverage.md`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.115.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.115.0)): Stage admits ESM v21 models. Concepts that use `generate pattern`, `generate random` or `generate rule` are refused with `STAGE-ESM-028`, naming the strategy, until Stage renders them. (part of #414)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.115.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.115.0)): `validate checksum` rules are refused with the new code `STAGE-ESM-042` until Stage renders them. (part of #414)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): Eight foundation screen templates in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default` under `foundation/templates`, category `Foundation / <type>`: `WorkspaceScreen` (`moduleNav`, `moduleContent`), `DashboardScreen`, `ListScreen` (header, toolbar, filters, list, bulkBar, footer), `BoardScreen`, `MasterDetailScreen` (fits the qualified slot `WorkspaceScreen.moduleContent`, offers the `master-detail.detail` outlet), `RecordPage` (tabs, related, activity, aside), `FormScreen` and `WizardScreen`. Each fits the `AppShell` `content` slot (or its container's qualified slot), so any of them is placed in the shell without per-template shell code; each carries a compact arrangement (list to cards, master/detail to a stack, section rail to tabs), the five standard states through the foundation components, permission rules on action controls, address-reflected filters/sort/page/selection/step, and a keyboard alternative on the board.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): Preview data is the sample-data pack `sampleData/foundation-en.json` (templates only declare query bindings), a thumbnail for each in `thumbnails/`, and the pack and thumbnails ship in the package.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): `verifyTemplateDefinitionOfDone` in `@cratis/scene.engine`: the Part B 6 per-template checklist (metadata, type, category, one-sentence description, thumbnail in the package, every slot arranged and none undeclared, placed by the slot resolver, no inline sample rows, every bound query answered by the pack), with specs and used for each foundation template.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): Gallery screens for the eight templates (breadcrumbs, nesting through the workspace for the qualified slot), the preview host takes a sample-data pack and forced state.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): Docs: `blueprints/foundation-templates.md`, `template-set.md` and `pattern-coverage.md` (Board, Record page and Wizard no longer deferred).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `dataList`: the records of a collection as a table, as cards, or grouped with subtotals, with search, sort, paging, selection (one or many), row actions and a filter (the filter builder's value). Search, sort, page and selection live in the address under a `urlKey` prefix, so a shared link or a refresh restores the list. Sorting cycles ascending, descending, none, and the header says so in words and in `aria-sort`. A table becomes cards on a phone, with nothing hidden. Real table elements: a screen reader announces row and column. Cells are text, number, currency, date (all locale-formatted) or status (tone + icon + text).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `tabSet` (the ARIA tabs pattern, the active tab in the address, the content of tab `overview` is the slot `overview`) and `subNavigation` (a module's sections as tabs or a left rail, `aria-current`, a deep link restores the section).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `bulkBar`: appears only while records are selected, says how many politely, offers bulk actions with permission rules, clears the selection.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `globalSearch` (a `search` landmark; text is the output and can live in the address; Escape clears) and `notificationsButton` (the unread count said in words, the badge hidden from assistive technology).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `kanbanBoard`: columns mapped to a status with cards that move by dragging **and** by each card's own "Move to" menu (the keyboard and screen-reader path), every move announced, per-column limits that say "Over the limit" in words, collapsible columns, read-only without the move permission; a card whose column is gone is shown in the first column rather than lost.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `wizardProgress`: linear steps, Back/Next/Finish and Save draft; Next is blocked while the step is not valid; the last step is the review; completed steps are revisitable but a step cannot be opened before those ahead are done; the current step (address) and completed steps (session) survive a refresh and a refresh cannot land past a step that was not done. Storage access is guarded, so a sandboxed frame loses only the memory (the Chromium audit caught this).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `formScreenBody`: titled sections in a 1-3 column grid with eleven field types (text, number, currency, date, select, multi-select, lookup, toggle, file, rich text, address), inline validation on blur and a summary on submit that takes focus, server errors mapped onto fields (an error for an unknown field becomes a general one, never lost), required marked by asterisk **and** word, four modes (create, edit, read-only, settings with a save per section), a sticky action bar, and an unsaved-changes guard (`beforeunload` + Discard). It collapses to one column on a phone.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): Shared: `useUrlState` (state that lives in the address without a history entry per keystroke) and `useCompactWidth`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): Descriptors, categories, stories, specifications for every model and control; all nine in the every-state jsdom audit and in the Chromium audit at desktop, tablet and phone in both themes (169 browser checks pass).
- **cli** ([Release v3.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.66.0)): `cratis render` admits Screenplay ESM v20: validation rules on generated concepts render with both the concept's `New()` factory and its validator. Specifications using such a concept must pin its generated value; a pin that fails the rules is refused with `STAGE-ESM-011`, and warning or information rules on generated concepts with `STAGE-ESM-038`. ESM v21 is still refused with `CLI-RENDER-004`. (#372)
- **cli** ([Release v3.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.66.0)): When Stage's default `eventmodel` engine refuses a model only for capabilities the semantic engine runs, `cratis run` without `--engine` restarts once on `semantic` and says so. An explicit `--engine` never switches; refusal suggestions give the exact `cratis run <path> --engine semantic` command with your other options. (part of #324)
- **cli** ([Release v3.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.66.0)): `cratis run` refusals are grouped by kind into **Fix your model**, **Not supported by this engine yet** and **Ignored by this engine**, with counts and the first three locations; `--verbose` lists every issue, and JSON output keeps every issue plus the groups. (part of #324)
- **cli** ([Release v3.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.66.0)): `cratis run` shows the compiler notes Stage reports as ignored: a one-line count when the session is ready (listed with `--verbose`), grouped under **Ignored by this engine** on a refusal, and as a raw `notes` array in JSON output.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): **Manifest** (`ScenePackage.blueprint`, C# `ScenePackage.Blueprint`): `BlueprintMetadata` - `domain`, `description`, `thumbnail`, `screenSet` (named `BlueprintScreen`s with template, parent, order and icon, i.e. the navigation) and `defaultSampleData`. Sample-data packs stay the existing `sampleData` references. `TemplateMetadata.thumbnail` for each template. There is deliberately no second version on the blueprint: the package `version` is the blueprint's version. The shared `scene-model-shape.json` keeps C# and TypeScript in step.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): **`verifyBlueprintPackage`** (C# `BlueprintVerification.Verify`), the definition of done: manifest kind and version; domain slug, description (at most 200 characters), thumbnail path and presence in the package; a non-empty screen set with unique names, titles, existing templates, parents in the set, no ancestor cycle and every screen provided; sample data present, references valid and in the package, a declared default when there are several; every template with display name, description, type from the vocabulary, a `Business / <domain>` category and a thumbnail, on top of the provenance `describeTemplateCatalog` already checks; every screen template placed by the slot resolver (unplaced, ambiguous and self-nested are reported); a dependency on the foundation set. A package with no `blueprint` block (a foundation blueprint) is held to the template, placement and sample-data rules only, or to all of them with `requireBlueprintMetadata`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): **Version pin semantics**: `evaluateBlueprintPin` (C# `BlueprintVersionPin.Evaluate`) decides Current / UpdateAvailable / UpdateBlockedByPin / NotSatisfied / Invalid for a reference's pinned range against the installed and latest versions; `defaultBlueprintPin` is the caret range of the installed version. `compareVersions` / `PackageVersionRange.CompareVersions` are new in both engines.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): **Gallery data**: `describeBlueprintCatalog` (C# `BlueprintCatalog.Describe`) and the `BlueprintCatalogFile` shape (`blueprintCatalog.json`): per domain blueprint its description, thumbnail, version, license, dependencies, screen set in navigation order, templates with type, category and thumbnail, and sample-data packs with the default marked. Every domain blueprint ships its entry; the repository root `blueprintCatalog.json` collects them.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): **`blueprint-verification-fixtures.json`**, a shared corpus (27 verification cases, 5 catalog cases, 17 pin cases, 8 version comparisons) asserted by `Engine.Specs/for_Blueprints` (C#) and `engine/for_blueprints` (TypeScript), plus a specification that fails when a rule has no case.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): **Scaffold**: `yarn new-blueprint <domain> ["Name"] ["Description"]` writes `Source/JavaScript/blueprint.<domain>` (templates, screens, sampleData, thumbnails, manifest, bundle, provenance and the verifying specification). What it writes is already done.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): **CI**: `yarn verify-blueprints` fails when a blueprint package has no verifying specification, when its packed archive lacks `thumbnails/`, `sampleData/` or the catalog or carries a thumbnail outside `thumbnails/`, and when the root catalog is stale. The build also scaffolds a package, builds, lints, tests and verifies it unchanged, so the generator cannot rot. The default and components blueprints verify themselves with the same function.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): Docs: `blueprints/ship-your-own-blueprint.md` - the domain blueprint layout, manifest, definition of done, version pins and catalog.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.38.0)): `SceneNavigationModel.moduleOf(screen)`: the host says which module a screen belongs to. Absent, the first segment of the qualified id decides, as before.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): `approvalChainStepper`: the steps a request goes through, who decides each and where it stands. The chain applies its own rules, so a request page needs none: a rejection stops the chain and the steps after it read "Not reached"; with no step marked current the first undecided step is the current one; only one step is current. The overall outcome is stated, every step says its state in words as well as by icon and color, the current step carries `aria-current="step"`, and the list reads vertically on a phone.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): `orgTree`: an organization as an expandable tree built from flat rows (`id`, `label`, `title`, `parentId`) as a query returns them - someone whose manager is missing becomes a root and a reporting cycle is cut instead of looping. It follows the ARIA tree pattern with one tab stop and the full keyboard set, says how many reports each person has in words, and publishes the selected id as `value`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): `permissionMatrix`: roles across, permissions down (optionally grouped), a checkbox where they meet, named "Grant *permission* to *role*"; every header is a real header cell. Grants of a role or permission that does not exist are ignored; changes are published as `value`. Without the edit permission each cell says "Allowed" or "Not allowed" in words and by icon. Wide matrices scroll in a keyboard-focusable region with the permission column kept in view.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): Descriptors, categories, stories (every state, light and dark, a rejected chain, a read-only matrix), docs, and specifications for the chain rules, the tree building and the grant handling.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): CI: the JavaScript build now installs Chromium and runs `yarn workspace @cratis/scene.blueprint.default test:browser`, which audits every foundation component in every state at desktop, tablet and phone widths in the light and dark themes with colour contrast and page overflow (Part B 6), so a regression fails the build.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.36.0)): `checklistSection`: sections of tasks with owner and due date, a labelled progress bar per section ("2 of 5 complete" is read, not only seen), "Overdue" said in words for an open task past its due day, ticking publishes the done task ids as `value`; read-only without the edit permission.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.36.0)): `activityFeed`: what happened to a record, grouped under a heading for each calendar day in a chosen time zone (newest first, or `order: ascending`), each entry naming its kind in words as well as by icon; times and days formatted for the locale. The application supplies the content; the component only lays it out.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.36.0)): `avatarStack`: overlapping avatars with initials or a picture, a "+N" chip announced as "N more", the stack labelled "N people".
- **Scene** ([Release v4.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.36.0)): `fileUploader`: a drop zone and a labelled button over a real file input (keyboard and screen-reader operable), checked against `accept`, `maxSizeMb` and `multiple` before anything is sent, with the reason for each refusal announced. The selection is published as `value`. Sending goes through the host's `FileUploaderHandlerProvider` - the same `(files, serverUrl) => Promise<void>` signature the PrimeReact `fileUpload` uses, so one host function serves both - with uploading, done and failed (with retry) states in words.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.36.0)): Descriptors, categories, stories (every state, light and dark), docs, specifications for each model (reading, grouping, overdue, selection checking) and each control, and the Chromium audit on desktop, tablet and phone in both themes.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.35.0)): `ApplicationHost.scope(navigation, children)`: what a host provides around everything that renders - the screen on the page and the dialog over it - inside the navigation providers.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.35.0)): `ApplicationHost.renderDialogTemplate(template, content, navigation)`: the host draws a dialog template the document declares, in place of the plain one; called inside `scope`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.35.0)): What a host draws through `renderScreen` and `renderDialogTemplate` runs as part of the application, so a hook it uses runs under the providers `scope` put in place.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): `chartWrapper`: line, bar, stacked bar, donut and funnel charts drawn as scalable vector graphics from the theme tokens (no chart library, so it renders in Stage, Scene and Studio alike). The drawing is hidden from assistive technology and followed by a table of the same numbers ("Chart data"); series are told apart by marker shape, dash and hatch as well as color; the chart draws at the width it is shown at, so labels keep their size on a phone. The scale, donut and funnel arithmetic are pure functions with their own specifications.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): `filterBuilder`: field/operator/value conditions joined by AND or OR in groups nested three deep, operators by field type. The filter is published as the `value` output (`{ operator, conditions, groups }`); a bound-in `filter` is read back with conditions on unknown fields dropped and operators repaired. Read-only without the edit permission.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): `columnChooser`: show/hide and reorder columns with buttons (no drag-and-drop needed), locked columns, Escape closes and returns focus. Publishes the ordered visible ids as `value`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): `savedViewsMenu`: the ARIA menu pattern (arrows, Home/End, Enter, Escape, focus returns to the button), the chosen view as `value`, and "Save current view" / "Manage views" on the `action` output, each with its own enabled/disabled/hidden permission rule.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): Descriptors, categories, stories (every state, light and dark, every chart kind), docs in `blueprints/foundation-components.md`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): Chromium audit extended to the new components, including every disclosure open, on desktop, tablet and phone in both themes.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): Foundation components in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default` under `foundation/`: `emptyState`, `statCard`, `trendChip` and `statusChip`, registered, categorised (CMP1: Feedback / Data display), described with property descriptors (data properties accept a query result, a data context or a component output - they declare a shape, never values) and iconed.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): The five standard states of Part B 1.4 as one frame, `FoundationFrame`: a skeleton while loading (announced, `prefers-reduced-motion` respected), an explanation with the primary action when empty, a message with a retry on error, what is missing and none of the data when the user has no permission, and the loaded part with the failed regions named when partial. A `state` property forces a state for previews; otherwise the state follows `loading`, `error`, `forbidden` and `failedRegions` and whether the component has data.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): Cross-cutting rules of Part B 1.5 in shared helpers: every text a component says comes from a strings table merged with the element's `strings` (localization), numbers, currency and dates are formatted with `Intl` for the element's `locale`, direction-sensitive CSS uses logical properties (right to left), `density` comfortable/compact, and every action control takes an enabled/disabled/hidden permission rule (`ActionButton`; disabled stays visible and says why).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): Theme tokens `status.{success,warning,danger,info}{Color,Background}` in both shipped themes, and the foundation section of the stylesheet, built only from theme tokens and one spacing scale.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): `FoundationStates` and Storybook stories showing every state in the light and dark themes (and right to left).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): Specifications: every component in every state audited with axe-core (WCAG 2.2 AA), no data leaked in the no-permission state, state derivation, strings, locale, right to left, permission rules, and a stylesheet scan that fails on a hard-coded color or spacing. `yarn test:browser` runs a Chromium audit at desktop, tablet and phone widths in both themes with colour contrast enabled (it found and fixed a contrast failure on the trend comparison).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): Docs: `blueprints/foundation-components.md`, status tokens in `blueprints/theme-tokens.md`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.181.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.181.0)): A model can declare `reaction batches atomic`: each trigger occurrence's direct `produces`, and each selected refusal branch's `produces`, then append all or none, so a rejected production admits none of the batch's facts, matching how Chronicle appends a reactor's returned events; invoked commands stay separate, and earlier batches and commands are kept when a later one fails (#769)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.181.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.181.0)): Executable model version 22 carries the declaration as `reactionProductionMode: "atomic"`; models without it keep per-fact production and their version, bytes and outcomes (#769)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.181.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.181.0)): `PLAY0693` reports a duplicate or conflicting `reaction batches` declaration (#769)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): `ScenePreviewHost({ document, structure, navigation, target, sampleData, registry, invoker, ... })`: a module target renders the application shell with the module's effective navigation and its first feature, a feature target that feature in the shell, a State Change slice target its task dialog over a minimal host page (the feature's own page when it has one), a State View slice target its data fragment. Navigation keeps to an in-memory history, so a preview never touches the hosting page's address, and going to another module swaps the effective navigation (`effectiveNavigation`, not re-implemented). It reports `onBreadcrumbChange` (`Sales › Orders`) and calls `onExit` on Escape when no dialog is open - the strip itself is the embedding application's.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): Read-only and never blank (PRV8): `diagnosePreview` judges the model with the engine's own validators and returns `PreviewProblem` entries (`code`, `severity`, `message`, `nodeId`). Errors (`targetNotFound`, `moduleWithoutFeatures`, `sliceNotPreviewable`, `screenMissing`, `layoutMissing`, `templateMissing`, `templateUnplaced`) replace the render with a problem list; warnings (`structureInvalid`, `navigationInvalid`, `toolbarInvalid`, `sampleDataInvalid`, `sliceFragmentMissing`) are reported and the preview renders. `onProblems` and `onSelectProblem` are the host's hooks.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): Dry run (PRV5): a submitted dialog validates and sends nothing; a toast says "Command would be sent" with the payload in an expandable detail. A real `invoker` can be passed instead.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): Sample data (PRV6): `createPreviewSampleData` asks `resolveSampleData` - bound pack, the application profile's active pack, the blueprint's default pack, generated data - for the queries the document binds and the commands its slices accept; `sessionActivePack` is the strip's session-only override of the profile's choice. The same seed gives the same rows.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): One composition for preview and Stage (PRV9, REN2): `composeApplicationScreen` (shell, chrome, the screen inside its template chain with every arrangement applied, name resolution, effective configuration), `composeOutletScreen`, `composeDialog`, `collectDestinations`, `previewProfile`, and `ApplicationHost`, which runs it - shell, nested outlets, navigation regions, routes and dialogs. A host decides only what is its own through `CompositionOptions` and `renderScreen`. The template wrapper (`Scene:template`) and the flow arrangement view Stage kept to itself now live here, so Stage can delete its copies.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): `SliceCommandField.initialValue`: a task dialog's field can start with a value - a preview fills it from the active sample data pack.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): Docs: `Documentation/blueprints/preview-host.md`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.113.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.113.0)): `GET /stage/status` returns an optional `notes` list with the compiler's information and warning diagnostics (code, message, source and severity), so clients can show what the engine ignored without treating it as a failure. (#430)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.71.0)): Declare a source-keyed, implicitly passive read model with `[EventStream<TSource>(stream)]`, read its stream instance through `IReadModels`, and protect decisions within the declared route. (#4932)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.71.0)): CHR0064 identifies invalid stream declarations and attribute misuse, with a monthly-withdrawal guide showing guarded decisions and plain queries. (#4932)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.102.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.102.0)): The Screenplay generator now recovers per-production `for` and `stream` routes for commands that return `EventForEventSourceId` wrappers, as single results, collections, tuples, or mixed with plain events, so each production keeps its own event source and stream in the generated model instead of the command's default route. Wrappers whose destination cannot be proven (for example copies made with `with`) are withheld and keep reporting SP0051 or SP0054. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3299)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.111.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.111.0)): Stage converts Screenplay's `sample data` packs and a `ui profile`'s `sample data` selection to Scene's sample data model. A pack becomes a `SampleDataPack` owned by the user, with a lower-case hyphenated id derived from its name (`NordicDemo` becomes `nordic-demo`), its title, locale and rows with the authored literals; the profile's active pack, shape mappings and read model bindings are carried as the profile's `SampleDataSelection`. `active blueprint <id>` keeps the blueprint's id as written and is resolved by the build. Sample data is design and preview time only and never reaches a production build. (#406)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.111.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.111.0)): The render plan reports Scene's own sample data validation as `SampleDataInvalid` findings, for example two pack names that derive the same id or an invalid locale. See the [render plan findings](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/render-plans.md#findings).
- **Stage** ([Release v4.111.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.111.0)): The Billing sample declares a pack and selects it once in the desktop profile.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.110.0)): Stage converts a Screenplay application's `navigation topbar|sidebar` regions to Scene's navigation model: the application's regions and logo from the layout, and each module's `inherits`/`extends`/`overrides` region, with item targets, slots, icons and sub-items. A document without regions serializes exactly as before. (part of #406)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.110.0)): Toolbar `button`s become toolbar items that carry the slice, emphasis and icon the Scene engine reads, with the slice written as its qualified id.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.110.0)): Template assignments, own and blueprint with their version range, are carried as Scene template references, and the slice kinds of a `scopes slice` template travel in its metadata.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.110.0)): The render plan reports Scene's own structure, navigation and toolbar validation as findings - `StructureInvalid`, `NavigationInvalid` and `ToolbarButtonInvalid` - for example a navigation target that does not exist, or a toolbar button that names a State View slice. See the [render plan findings](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/render-plans.md#findings).
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.25.0)): `GenerateValue.Pattern(pattern, checksum)` and `GenerateValue.Pattern(pattern, context, checksum)` generate a value from a pattern such as `ORD-[A-HJ-NP-Z2-9]{4}-#{4,6}` or `INV-{yyyy}-#{7}`. Every distinct value the pattern allows is equally likely, date segments come from `context.Now` in UTC, and an optional `Checksum` (`Luhn`, `Mod11`, `Mod97`) appends check digits; `Mod11` never produces the excluded check value 10. See [Patterns](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/blob/main/Documentation/csharp/generating-values/patterns.md). (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.25.0)): `GenerateValue.Integer(minimum, maximum)` draws an unbiased number from an inclusive `long` range, including the full `long` range. (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.25.0)): `GenerateValue.HasValidChecksum(value, checksum)` checks that a value ends with the correct Luhn, mod 11 or ISO 7064 MOD 97-10 check digits. (part of #1176)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.0)): Top-level `sample data <Name>` block: optional `title`, optional BCP 47 `locale`, and `shape <ReadModel>` blocks of `row` blocks with `<field> = <literal>` lines (string, number, `true`, `false`, `null`). Design and preview time only: binding reports it as deferred (`PLAY0269`), it adds nothing to the executable model or ESM output and is never part of a production build.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.0)): The `ui profile` gains an optional `sample data` block: `active <Pack>` or `active blueprint <id>`, the rare `shape <Shape> maps to <ReadModel>` and `bind <ReadModel> to <Pack>`. The active pack is chosen once, there, for the whole application; there is no per-screen, per-slice or per-component selector (decision 0081, which supersedes the spec's per-screen placement).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY0702`-`PLAY0715`: invalid or duplicate pack, repeated title, locale, shape, field or selection line, a shape naming no read model (warning), a field that is not a property of the read model, a literal that does not fit the property type, an active, mapped or bound pack or read model the document does not declare.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.0)): The canonical printer, the syntax walker and index, the folder merge, the typed JSON schema, the contract catalogue (`sample` construct, admission probe), the MCP `read-ast`/`propose-ast` typed nodes (`SampleDataPackSyntax`, `SampleDataSelectionSyntax`, `SampleDataSetSyntax`, `SampleDataRowSyntax`, `SampleDataFieldSyntax`, ...) and the canonical screen composition corpus all carry sample data.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.0)): Monaco (highlighting, hover, completion in the root, the pack, its shapes and the profile block) and the VS Code grammar and spelling dictionary know the new words; the TypeScript compiler skips the block whole.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.0)): Documentation page `sample-data.md` (grammar, `ui-profile.md`, `diagnostics.md`), the Invoicing sample and decision 0081.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.179.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.179.0)): Toolbar buttons that invoke State Change slices. A screen `toolbar` accepts `button <id> title "<text>" [slice <Module>.<Feature>.<Slice>] [emphasis primary|secondary|quiet] [icon <name>]` beside its `item` entries - the Screenplay form of Scene's `ToolbarButton`. The slice names the command and the dialog that opens over the page, so the button needs no command of its own. `slice` is a dotted path or any unambiguous suffix; the title is a quoted string or a `$strings.<key>` token. See [Toolbar buttons](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/screens.md#toolbar-buttons).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.179.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.179.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY0760`-`PLAY0765`: a malformed button line, a slice the document does not declare, a slice that is not a State Change slice (a button invokes State Change slices only), a button without a slice (warning), an ambiguous slice reference, and a duplicate id. They mirror Scene's `toolbarButtonSliceNotFound`, `toolbarButtonSliceNotStateChange` and `toolbarButtonSliceEmpty`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.179.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.179.0)): The syntax tree carries `ToolbarButtonSyntax` on `ScreenToolbarSyntax.Buttons`; it is omitted from the JSON when empty, so existing documents keep their bytes. The printer round-trips every button, and the TypeScript compiler reads buttons into the same tree with a shared conformance vector (`toolbar-buttons`).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.179.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.179.0)): Monaco and VS Code highlight, document and complete buttons; the Invoicing sample uses them.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.15.0)): `@StartAtTail` annotation for reactor classes. The first time Chronicle sees the reactor, it begins at the tail of the event sequence and observes only what is appended afterwards. A reactor that already has a position keeps it. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/blob/main/Documentation/reference/annotations.md#startattail. (part of Cratis/Direct#1875)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.53.0)): `@startAtTail()` class decorator for reactors. The first time Chronicle sees the reactor, it begins at the tail of the event sequence and observes only what is appended afterwards. A reactor that already has a position keeps it. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/blob/main/Documentation/reactors.md#start-at-the-tail. (part of Cratis/Direct#1875)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.14.0)): `start_at_tail: true` option on `use Chronicle.Reactors.Reactor`. The first time Chronicle sees the reactor, it begins at the tail of the event sequence and observes only what is appended afterwards. A reactor that already has a position keeps it. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/blob/main/Documentation/reactors.md#starting-at-the-tail. (part of Cratis/Direct#1875)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.178.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.178.0)): Navigation regions in the language. A `layout` declares the application's `navigation topbar` and `navigation sidebar` with an optional `logo image "<path>" title "<text>"` and `item <id> title "<text>" [icon <name>] [target <Module>[.<Feature>]] [slot <name>]` lines; sidebar items nest sub-items. A `module` declares only what it changes: `navigation sidebar extends`, `navigation topbar overrides`, or `inherits` (the default when absent). The blocks are the Screenplay form of Scene's `NavigationModel`; `contribute to Navigation` stays valid and independent. See the new [Navigation regions](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/navigation.md) page.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.178.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.178.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY0740`-`PLAY0752` for malformed blocks and items, duplicate regions, application-only logos, modes where they are not allowed, topbar sub-items, and the findings that mirror Scene's `navDuplicateId`, `navTargetMissing`, `navSlotUnknown`, `navTargetOutOfScope` and `navModuleWithoutFeatures`, plus a warning for an override without items.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.178.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.178.0)): The syntax tree carries `NavigationRegionSyntax`, `NavigationItemSyntax`, `NavigationTargetSyntax` and `NavigationLogoSyntax` on `LayoutSyntax.Navigation` and `ModuleSyntax.Navigation`. They are omitted from the JSON when empty, so existing documents keep their bytes. The printer round-trips every item, id, icon, slot, target and mode; the executable model reports the blocks as deferred (`PLAY0269`).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.178.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.178.0)): Monaco and VS Code highlight, document and complete the new words and blocks; the Invoicing sample uses them.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): `template blueprint <Blueprint> <Name> [version "<range>"]` references a template a blueprint package ships, beside the own `template <Name>`. It is valid wherever `template` is: as a module, feature or slice assignment and inside a screen.
