The optional `version` is the semver range of the blueprint the author accepts - Scene's subset (`*`, an exact version, `^`, `~`, `>=`, `>`, `<=`, `<`), the same one Scene's `TemplateReference.version` and `resolveTemplateReference` use. `PLAY0721` reports a range Scene cannot read; `PLAY0722` reports a blueprint no `ui profile` lists under `packages` (checked only when a profile declares packages). Version drift and unknown blueprint templates are reported where the blueprint is installed (Scene `TemplateReferenceResolver`), because the compiler cannot see a package's catalog.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): `scopes slice statechange`, `scopes slice stateview` and `scopes slice statechange|stateview` narrow a slice-scoped template to slice kinds - Scene's `TemplateMetadata.sliceKinds`. Using a restricted template on an excluded slice kind, or on an automation or translate slice (which present nothing), is `PLAY0631`. The conformance vector `when_checking_template_applicability_against_the_scene_vectors` restates the cases of Scene's shared `template-applicability-fixtures.json` (`ApplicableTemplates`) as Screenplay source.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): `PLAY0720` (warning): a `layout`, `screen template` or `dialog template` without a `category`. A blueprint package build treats warnings as errors, so there it fails; an own template only warns. (#761)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): The new members round-trip through the C# and TypeScript compilers, the printer (including comments on `category`), the syntax schema and the MCP contract; keywords, hover documentation and diagnostics reach Monaco and the VS Code grammar; the TypeScript compiler and C# are held to the same `template-references` conformance vector. (#761)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.70.0)): `[StartAtTail]` attribute for .NET reactors. The first time Chronicle sees the reactor, it begins at the tail of the event sequence and observes only what is appended afterwards. A reactor that already has a position keeps it, so the attribute is safe to leave in place. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/reactors/start-at-tail.mdx. (part of Cratis/Direct#1875)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.70.0)): Optional `StartAtTail` field on the reactor definition in the gRPC contract. Clients that do not send it behave as before.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.109.0)): Stage admits ESM v20 models: a concept that declares `generate` may carry validation rules, and the rendered concept has both its `New()` factory and its `ConceptValidator`. ESM v21 is still refused. (#393)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.109.0)): A rendered specification that uses a rule-bearing generated concept must pin its generated value, and a pinned value that fails the concept's rules is refused with `STAGE-ESM-011`. (#393)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.109.0)): A generated concept rule with warning or information severity is refused with `STAGE-ESM-038`, because the rendered application only rejects error-severity failures. (#393)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `SliceInvoker` (`invoke(sliceId, payload, command)` returning an `InvocationResult` with `succeeded`, `validationErrors`, `message`, `payload`, `dryRun`), `SliceInvokerProvider` and `useSliceInvoker`. Without a provider the command is announced as `cratis.scene.command`, as a toolbar action always was (`createAnnouncingInvoker`).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `createDryRunInvoker({ onInvoked, validate })`: validates, sends nothing and returns a result carrying the would-be payload, for the Studio preview's toast (PRV5).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `SliceDialogView` and the `core:sliceDialog` component: a modal card with the button title, the subtitle `State Change · <Slice>`, a labelled field per command property, `Cancel` and a submit button labelled with the title. The first field takes focus, Tab stays inside, Escape cancels and focus returns to the button. Required fields are checked before invoking, and the validation errors an invoker reports are shown against their fields (`aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby`) or for the whole form. Success closes the dialog and signals the result.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `SliceCommandFieldsProvider`: the host tells the dialog a command's fields, so `@cratis/scene.react` stays free of Arc and transport imports.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `core:toolbarItem` resolves its slice against the navigation model, opens the dialog `sliceDialogFor` picked through the navigation host (`sliceDialogDestination`; an authored `dialogs` template wins over the built-in dialog), carries its `emphasis` as `data-scene-emphasis` and `scene-toolbar-button-<emphasis>`, and shows a warning badge for a button without a slice only when the host asks (`SliceInvokerProvider warnEmptySlices`, implied by a dry-run invoker). Without a navigation model it announces the command as before.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `SceneNavigationHost` carries the dialog's parameters (`currentDialogParameters`) and names the dialog after its title.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `blueprint.default` styles the primary, secondary and quiet buttons, the warning badge and the dialog over its scrim, and has a Storybook story.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): Docs: "Running a slice button: the task dialog and the `sliceInvoker`" in `blueprints/application-hierarchy.md`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): `NavigationRegionView` in `@cratis/scene.react`, driven by `effectiveNavigation(...)` for the module of the screen on the page, so a module swap recomputes the items in place without remounting the `nav`. A sidebar nests, collapses and expands the parents of the active item (`expand='all'` shows everything); a topbar is flat.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): Active state follows the navigation host: an item is active (`aria-current`, `data-active`) when the screen it opens is shown in the slot it renders into, so items rendering into different slots are active at once.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): Render-into-slot: activating an item calls `navigate` with `navigationItemDestination(item)` (module/feature identity, screen, slot as `outlet`). `navigationDestinations(...)` lists every destination so deep links resolve.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): `SceneNavigationHost` takes `navigationModel` (structure, navigation, `screenFor`, `isVisible`) and a `frame` that wraps the primary outlet in chrome that stays mounted across navigation; `SceneNavigationModelProvider` does the same without a host.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): Hooks for the layers around it: `isVisible(item)` hides items (never disables), items carry `data-origin` / `data-read-only`, and `renderAdornment(item)` lets Studio draw the lock of an inherited item. No design chrome is rendered.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): Keyboard: roving tab stop, arrows (left/right in a topbar, up/down in a sidebar), Home/End, and right/left to expand/collapse a sidebar parent.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): `blueprint.default` `Topbar` and `Sidebar` show the region as the shell menu (the `layout-menu` classes, with the active underline and pill of the specification) when a navigation model is in scope and the element has no `menu` / `content` slot of its own; `regionNavigation: false` opts out. A Storybook story shows the hierarchical sidebar and two slots.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): Docs: "Rendering the regions" in `blueprints/navigation-regions.md`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.176.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.176.0)): Warning PLAY0694 when a Translate slice without `direction` has a reaction (without a `from` route) to an event that a capture in the same slice appends, including appends in capture children and nested scopes. The message names the events and tells you to keep the capture in the Translate slice and move the reaction to an Automation slice; there is no automatic repair. Ordinary compilation is unaffected, but builds that treat warnings as errors (for example `--warnaserror`) fail on such models until the slice is split. Both compilers and the editors report it. See [decision 0079](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0079-warn-when-a-directionless-translate-slice-reacts-to-its-own-capture.md) and [PLAY0694](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/diagnostics.md). (#760)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.69.0)): Kernel occurrence queue: triggers register with a JSON payload schema, `RecordOccurrence` stores each occurrence once per idempotency id (`Recorded`, `AlreadyRecorded`, `OccurrenceIdConflict`, `OccurrencePayloadInvalid`), and workers claim, renew, complete and fail occurrences under leases fenced by an epoch. A superseded worker gets `OccurrenceLeaseLost`; a repeated completion gets `AlreadyCompleted`. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.69.0)): Failed occurrences back off and are quarantined after the configured maximum attempts without blocking later occurrences; `RetryQuarantinedOccurrence` re-arms one and records the authenticated principal. Retry settings live in the new `Occurrences` configuration section, documented at https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/configuration/chronicle-options.mdx. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.69.0)): A disconnected client's leases expire, and occurrence queues resume after a kernel restart. `GetOccurrence` and `ObserveOccurrences` report state, attempts, lease, last error, next attempt and the receipts written for an occurrence, with their namespace. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.69.0)): Effect receipts: every append command (`Append`, `AppendMany`, `AppendManyForEventSources` and their named-tag variants) accepts an optional effect receipt that is written in the same storage transaction as the events. A repeated effect returns `EffectAlreadyRecorded` with the stored outcome and response and appends nothing. A zero-event append with concurrency scopes records a `NoEvents` receipt only when the scopes hold. `RecordEffectReceipt` records a `NoEvents` or `Refused` outcome on its own, and `GetEffectReceipt` reads one back. An unknown commit result is settled by lookup, or fails retryably with `EffectReceiptUnsettled`. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/concepts/effect-receipts.md. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.69.0)): The kernel advertises the `occurrences` and `effect-receipts` capabilities, and the new `Occurrences` gRPC service is part of the generated Kotlin, TypeScript and Elixir contracts. (part of #4911)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `ToolbarButton` (`id`, `title`, `slice`, `emphasis`, `icon`, `target`), `Toolbar` and `ToolbarEmphasis` (`primary`, `secondary`, `quiet`) in `Cratis.Scene.Model.Toolbar` and `@cratis/scene.model`, mirrored in `scene-model-shape.json`. A spec button is a `core:toolbarItem` with `kind: Action` plus `slice` and `emphasis`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `validateToolbarButtons` / `ToolbarButtonValidation.Validate` with `toolbarButtonSliceNotFound` and `toolbarButtonSliceNotStateChange` (errors) and `toolbarButtonSliceEmpty` (warning for a button without a slice).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `sliceDialogFor` / `SliceDialogs.For` and `resolveToolbarButton` / `ToolbarButtons.Resolve`: a slice-backed button resolves to its command and task dialog - the slice's own `dialogTemplate`, else the default `FormDialog` - so the dialog is decided once, in Scene.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `toolbarButtonFromItem` / `ToolbarButtons.FromItem` read a button from a `core:toolbarItem`'s properties, and `normalizeToolbarButton` converts a hand-authored `kind: Action` + `target` command to the one state change slice that accepts it. A command no single slice accepts stays a legacy button and still validates.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `SliceNode.command` and `SliceNode.dialogTemplate`, and `ApplicationStructureIndex.findSlicesByCommand`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): The `core:toolbarItem` descriptor: title, `slice` (editor `slice`, kinds `StateChange`), `emphasis` (enum chips), `icon` and the legacy `target`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `toolbar-slice-fixtures.json` (state change ok, state view and automation rejected, missing slice, empty slice warning, dialog template resolution, conversion from a `target` command, reading buttons from items) asserted by both `Engine.Specs/for_ToolbarButtonValidation` and `engine/for_validateToolbarButtons`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): The toolbar section of `blueprints/application-hierarchy.md` documents the button, validation, resolution and compatibility.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.174.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.174.0)): `RuleConsistency.Check(SemanticConcept concept, int? utcAssessmentYear = null)` returns the compiler's proof status for each validation rule of a generated concept, in rule order, from a deserialized executable model. A renderer can let a concept whose rules are all Proven use unpinned generation and keep requiring pinned fixtures only for Unproven rules. The status is deliberately not serialized: it is derived from the concept and the UTC assessment year. See [proof status for renderers](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/generated-values/strategies.md#proof-status-for-renderers). (#775)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): `NavigationModel`, `ApplicationNavigation`, `ModuleNavigation`, `NavigationRegion` (`topbar`/`sidebar`, `inherit`/`extend`/`override`), `NavItem` (`id`, `title`, `icon`, `target`, `slot`, `children`) and `ApplicationLogo` in `Cratis.Scene.Model.Navigation` and `@cratis/scene.model`, mirrored in `scene-model-shape.json`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): `effectiveNavigation` / `EffectiveNavigation.Resolve`: the ordered items of a region for the application or a module, each with `origin` (application | module), `readOnly`, a `reason` and its target resolved against the application structure (a module target goes to the module's first feature), plus the region's effective `mode` and `sources` tag (`defined here`, `inherited`, `Application + Sales`, `Sales only`). Sub-items are sidebar-only.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): Edit helpers `switchRegionMode` (inherit clears the module items; inherit → override with `copy` copies the application items first), `addNavItem` (adding to an inheriting region switches it to extend first; sub-items under a parent) and `setApplicationLogo` (refused for a module), with `applyNavigationEdits`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): `diagnoseNavigationRegions` and the new diagnostic codes `navTargetMissing`, `navSlotUnknown`, `navDuplicateId`, `navModuleWithoutFeatures` and `navTargetOutOfScope`; `diagnoseNavigation` runs them when its graph carries `structure` and `navigation`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): The `replace` collection operation and `InstanceContribution.replaceOwnerItems`: an instance can hide the items it inherits so only its own remain. It is how `override` is expressed in the exposure model. Without `replace` exposed the contribution is ignored and reported, and the server check refuses a document that sets it.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): Shared fixtures `navigation-inheritance-fixtures.json` and `navigation-targets-fixtures.json`, the new codes in `navigation-diagnostic-fixtures.json` and two server cases in `document-change-fixtures.json`, asserted by both the C# and the TypeScript engines.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): The documentation page `blueprints/navigation-regions.md`; the exposure and navigation diagnostics pages are extended.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): Sample-data model in C# and TypeScript (and `scene-model-shape.json`): `SampleDataPack`, `SampleShape`, `SampleShapeField`, `SampleFieldKind`, `SampleDataSelection` and `ScenePackage.sampleData` (a Blueprint package ships its packs as `sampleData/*.json`).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `UiProfile.sampleData` selects the active pack once for the whole application (own or from a blueprint), plus the rare shape-to-read-model mappings and per-read-model bindings. There is no per-screen, slice or component selector; the preview strip's choice is the session-only `sessionActivePack` of the resolve context.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `resolveSampleData` / `SampleDataResolver.Resolve` implement the resolution order bound pack, UI profile active pack (or session override), blueprint default pack, generated data; nothing resolves unless `isDesignOrPreview` is true, so a Stage production build gets no sample data.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `generateSample` / `SampleDataGenerator.Generate`: a seeded xoshiro128** generator typed by field kind (ids, names, companies, emails, phones, cities, text, numbers, amounts, booleans, dates, enums), honouring nullability, `min`/`max` and pack locale (`en-GB`, `en-US`, `nb-NO`, `sv-SE`, `de-DE`), byte-identical in both engines for a seed.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `validateSampleDataPack`, `validateSampleDataSelection`, `validateSampleDataPackages` and `describeSampleDataCatalog` (C#: `SampleDataValidation`, `SampleDataCatalogs`) check what blueprint packages ship and build the selector content (generated, own, then each blueprint's group).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `findDescriptorSampleRowLiterals` / `findTemplateSampleRowLiterals` detect row literals in component descriptors and templates; `verify-package-contents.js` fails when sample data is packed outside `sampleData/`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `sample-data-resolution-fixtures.json`, asserted independently by `Engine.Specs/for_SampleData` and `engine/for_sampleData`, and the documentation page `blueprints/sample-data.md`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): `ApplicableTemplates.Compute` / `applicableTemplates(library, target)`: own templates by category, then only the blueprints that have at least one applicable template (TPL2). A target is a scope, an optional slice kind, the container whose slots the template must fit, and the chain of templates already chosen above (no loops, TPL6). It composes `TemplateApplicability` and `ScreenTemplateResolver` with the per-level defaults of spec 4.2; explicit `scopes`/`sliceKinds` override and category never implies a scope. `ApplicableTemplates.Validate` / `validate*ForTarget` give the reason a template does not apply.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): `TemplateReference` (`source` Own | Blueprint, `id`, `blueprint`, `version` semver range, default `^<installed>`) and `TemplateReferenceResolver.Resolve` / `resolveTemplateReference` with `Resolved`, `Malformed`, `UnknownBlueprint`, `VersionNotSatisfied` (drift) and `UnknownTemplate`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): `TemplateMetadata.sliceKinds` (reusing `SliceKind` from the application structure) narrows which slice kinds a template applies to.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): Catalog entries carry `warnings`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): `ComponentDescriptor` gains `category`, `icon` (an `IconReference`), `badge`, `keywords` and `designMode`. `ComponentCategory` holds the eight categories of the spec (Navigation, Data display, Input, Actions, Layout, Feedback, Overlay, Forms); a package may add its own.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): `ComponentDesignModeDescriptor` declares, as data, where a component's items are (`itemsPath`, a `Collection` property), the "add" affordances and the floating item toolbar. `availability` and `readOnlyReason` are rule names, never functions; every edit is an ordinary collection edit, so undo/redo and the dirty state need nothing of their own. `navigationDesignMode()` and `toolbarDesignMode()` build the actions of spec DM3.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): The property-editor vocabulary (`PropertyEditorKind`: `text`, `number`, `boolean`, `enum`, `select`, `color`, `icon`, `list`, `reference`, `navigationTarget`, `renderingSlot`, `slice`, `inheritanceMode`, `template`, `sampleData`, `field`, `columnList`, plus the structured kinds that already existed) and `PropertyDescriptor.editorOptions` (`multiline`, `placeholder`, `unit`, `step`, `kinds`, `reference` filter, `fieldKinds`, `tokensOnly`).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): Engine rules shared by every host: `validateComponentDescriptors` (`componentCategoryMissing` error, `componentCategoryUnknown` warning, `componentDesignModeInvalid`, `propertyEditorOptionsInvalid`), `matchComponentQuery` (the picker filter: name/keyword substring, category by word start, highlight ranges, keyword hint), `rankFrequentlyUsedComponents` (top 6, count then recency) and `componentBadge`. All are asserted against the shared corpus `component-metadata-fixtures.json`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): Every component of the core, Cratis.Components, PrimeReact and both blueprint packages (164 components) now has a descriptor with category, icon, keywords and display name; Topbar, Sidebar, Toolbar and the navigation bar declare a design mode. `validateComponentMetadata(bundle)` checks a bundle (CMP1) and is run by a specification in each package and by a repository-level specification (`registry.conformance`) that enumerates all package registries.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): The third-party fixture `Acme.Inspections` declares a category, icon, keywords and design mode using only public packages; documentation in `Documentation/editing/descriptors.md` and `design-time-extensions.md`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.23.0)): `ApplicationStructure`, `ModuleNode`, `FeatureNode`, `SliceNode` and `SliceKind` (`StateChange`, `StateView`, `Automation`) in `Cratis.Scene.Model.Structure` and `@cratis/scene.model`, mirrored in `scene-model-shape.json`. The structure is a reference input supplied by the host (Stage's converter, Studio); it is not persisted in a scene document.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.23.0)): `ApplicationStructureIndex` in `Cratis.Scene.Engine.Structure` and `engine/structure`: `findModule`, `findFeature`, `firstFeature`/`firstFeatureId`, `findSlice`, `kindOf`, `findFeaturesByName` and the diagnostics `structureDuplicateId` and `structureAmbiguousName`. Pure, no DOM.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.23.0)): `application-structure-fixtures.json` (qualified lookup, nested features, first feature, module without features, unknown/wrongly typed ids, same-named features in different modules, duplicate ids, ambiguous names) asserted by both `Engine.Specs/for_ApplicationStructureIndex` and `engine/for_applicationStructureIndex`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.23.0)): The documentation page `blueprints/application-structure.md` explains the input and who supplies it.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): The scaffold emits a `<Project>.Specs` project next to the web project. It compiles the generated slice specifications in place, so slice files keep their paths, and runs them with `dotnet test`. (part of #401)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): The scaffold enables central package management: `Directory.Packages.props` pins every package version and the projects reference packages without versions. (part of #401)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): The scaffold's `Directory.Build.props` sets nullable reference types, the latest C# version, warnings as errors and code-style enforcement for every project, and an `.editorconfig` that allows specification-style names in specifications. NuGet's machine-dependent restore warnings (`NU1507`, `NU1900`–`NU1904`) stay warnings. (part of #401)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): The scaffold adds a `README.md` with run instructions for Chronicle, the backend, the frontend and the specifications. (part of #401)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): Read protection for units of work: an enforcing unit owned by one invocation, protected decision reads that enrol before they return (`IDecisionReads.GetFor`), guards on every public read and append entry point, owner-only completion, read allowances for advisory reads, and the append conditions a commit sends (`GetAppendConditions`) (#4888)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): Protected decision reads of reducers and self-reducing read models, folding with the handler's declared event generation; filtered reducers and reducers with constructor dependencies are refused (#4888)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): `CHR0062` reports an event source definition or stream whose concurrency leaves out `EventSourceId`, with a code fix (#4888)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): `CHR0063` reports an explicit concurrency scope for one stream passed to an append routed to another stream of the same event source (#4899)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): A documentation page on read protection, and decision read documentation for reducers, pinned delivery and detached reads under read protection (#4888)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.101.0)): The Screenplay generator recovers a generated concept's validator rules together with its built-in generation strategy, using Screenplay ESM v20. Generated values, identities, responses, mappings and pins are no longer withheld just because the concept has a `ConceptValidator`.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.101.0)): Rules the compiler proves against the strategy are emitted.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.101.0)): Rules it cannot decide are kept and reported as `SP0078`.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.101.0)): A refuted rule withholds that concept's generation and is reported as `SP0073` with a counterexample.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.101.0)): Below an ESM v20 version cap, the generation is withheld but the rules are kept.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): Concepts can generate values from a `pattern` with digits, letters, ASCII classes, bounded repeats and UTC date segments (`{yyyy}`, `{yy}`, `{MM}`, `{dd}`), optionally followed by a `checksum luhn`, `mod11` or `mod97` check digit; generation draws uniformly from the exact set of values the pattern and checksum can produce, and a pattern whose set is empty for any date in the next hundred years, or too large to count, is a binding error (`PLAY0692`, `PLAY0691`) (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): Int concepts can generate a `random <min>..<max>` value, and `generate rule Name` names a value generator implemented in code, which receives the current time and tenant (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): `validate checksum luhn|mod11|mod97` checks a String concept's check digits, and is proven to hold for values the matching `checksum` strategy generates (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): Rules on concepts with these strategies are proven like the other strategies' rules, including length and `min`/`max` against `random`, and specification fixtures are checked against the pattern, its date segments (using `given clock`) and the checksum (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): Executable model version 21 carries these strategies and checksum validations; models without them keep their version, bytes and outcomes (part of #616)
- **cli** ([Release v3.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.65.0)): `cratis screenplay validate` reads the validation gate a repository declares in `screenplay.json` at the model root: the `checks`, `warningsAsErrors`, `scope` and `openQuestions` the standalone `screenplay` tool also reads, through the same Screenplay library. A command-line option overrides only its own setting, and a missing file keeps today's defaults. An invalid or unreadable file is refused with its location and exit 1 before anything compiles. See [the screenplay.json reference](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/tool.md). (#347)
- **cli** ([Release v3.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.65.0)): `cratis screenplay validate --print-gate` prints the effective gate and where each value came from without compiling, `--no-gate-file` ignores the file for one run, and the JSON result carries a `gate` object in the same shape as the standalone tool's. (#347)
- **cli** ([Release v3.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.65.0)): `cratis render` accepts Screenplay ESM v18 models: a specification that queries with an out-of-rule key or filter value and expects `then error` renders as a specification asserting the query's validation failure. Shapes Stage cannot reproduce exactly are refused with Stage diagnostics; ESM v20 models are still refused with `CLI-RENDER-004`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.171.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.171.0)): A specification drives a capture over events by presenting the consumed event itself: `given <Event>` and `when append <Event>` for an event a capture reads `from`. The capture keeps one state per key, folding earlier events in order, and appends what its rules map: the first event for a key counts as added, an event that changes nothing appends nothing, and `when <field>` and transitions hold only on a change. `for` on such a fixture is optional. See [capturing from public events](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/captures.md) and decision [0077](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0077-present-consumed-public-events-to-captures-over-events.md). (#483)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.106.0)): Stage admits Screenplay ESM v18. A specification that queries with an out-of-rule key or filter value and expects `then error` renders as a companion specification asserting the query's validation failure, and Stage's semantic runs and the Stage Host reproduce the reference's `Validation` rejection. (#370)
- **cli** ([Release v3.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.64.0)): `cratis run --engine <eventmodel|semantic>` starts the Stage sandbox on the chosen runtime engine. `semantic` runs the Screenplay executable model, enforcing validation, requirements, authorization, conditional production and constraints, and running models with named event sources and streams. Without the option, Stage's default engine runs as before. (#368)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.67.0)): Kernel storage for application occurrences in MongoDB, SQL and InMemory. A replayed ingress id is recognized, and a mismatched payload is refused. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.67.0)): Kernel storage for fenced occurrence leases. A worker whose lease epoch has been superseded can no longer renew, complete or fail the occurrence. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.67.0)): Kernel storage for schedule definitions and cursors. The start time is written together with the first registration, and each cursor moves forward in versioned, atomic turns. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.67.0)): Effect receipts stored in the same transaction as the events they cover, in every provider, including receipts for batches without events. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.67.0)): SQL migrations that create the occurrence, schedule and receipt tables when the kernel upgrades. (part of #4911)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.100.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.100.0)): The fresh-identifier guard now also covers events written directly through the command's unit of work: `IUnitOfWork.AddEvent`, `AddEventWithNamedTags` and `AddEvents`, and `eventLog.Transactional.Append`, `AppendMany` and their named-tag variants. It covers nested commands too. Writing to an occupied generated identifier fails with `ConcurrencyViolation` and `GeneratedIdentifierCollision`, and the whole unit of work is rolled back. See [Fresh identifiers](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/commands/fresh-identifiers.md). (#3245)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.100.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.100.0)): `ICommandExceptionMapper` lets an integration turn an exception thrown while a command runs into a `CommandResult`. Mappers are discovered by convention. (#3245)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.105.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.105.0)): A slice that declares screens now gets its own `Screens.scene.json` in its folder, listing that slice as its source, so a consumer such as Studio's slice Preview can show a slice's screens. The application's `scene.json` is assembled from the same fragments and stays byte-identical. Slice- and feature-scoped plans emit the selected slices' fragments, byte-identical to an application plan, and leave out the application-wide Scene files with an informational diagnostic, so a scoped write never leaves them partial. Screen ownership supplied by a caller is checked against the compiled model: an owner that doesn't declare the screen, or a stale screen payload, fails planning. (#400)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.66.0)): Event store subscriptions can now opt in to forward every event into the target inbox in the generation the target application declared. This applies to live forwarding, catch-up, replay and partition retries. Opt in with `WithGenerationDelivery(EventGenerationDelivery.Pinned)` on the .NET subscription builder; typed registrations send their declared generation, and `WithEventType(eventTypeId, generation)` names one explicitly. The kernel stores those generations with the subscription and selects, decrypts and, when needed, migrates each event to that generation before appending it to the inbox. Subscriptions that do not opt in are unchanged. See [explicit subscriptions](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/subscriptions/explicit-subscriptions.mdx) (part of #4710)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.99.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.99.0)): The Screenplay generator states a scenario run over a fixed set of literal cases once, as a Screenplay case table. An abstract base specification with primary-constructor parameters and body-less concrete rows (`class Ada() : Registering("Ada")`) becomes one specification with typed `parameter` lines and one `case` row per concrete class. Rows are folded only when substituting each row's values into the base reproduces that row's steps, outcomes and caller exactly; otherwise every row is stated individually as before and Information `SP0076` names why. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3197)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Decision reads for the dynamic consistency boundary. `Chronicle.ReadModels.DecisionReads` `get/2`, `get_detached/2`, `admit/2` and their stream-scoped variants enroll a read into the unit of work, and `Chronicle.decide/2` commits it, returning `{:error, %Chronicle.ReadModels.DecisionConflict{}}` when a concurrent append invalidated the read. Passive reducers can be read this way too. See [Dynamic Consistency Boundary](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/blob/main/Documentation/dynamic-consistency-boundary.md). (#140)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): `Chronicle.Testing`: an in-memory kernel with concurrency and constraint checks, in-process reactors and reducers, `wait_for_observers/1`, `assert_appended`/`refute_appended`, `Chronicle.Testing.Case`, an isolated-store helper for integration specs, and `Chronicle.Behaviour` so the `Chronicle` facade can be mocked with Mox. See [Testing](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/blob/main/Documentation/testing.md). (#149)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Captures: `use Chronicle.Captures.Capture` declares a capture that compiles to Capture Declaration Language, with compile-time errors for a missing source, key or append condition. `Chronicle.Captures` registers, starts, stops, lists and validates captures, and the client registers them through `captures:` or `otp_app:` discovery. (#148)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Discovery report and artifact identity drift: `Chronicle.Client.discovery_report/1`, `identity_drift/1`, a `drift_policy: :ignore | :warn | :fail` option (default `:warn`, never fails startup), a one-time warning for reactor, reducer and projection ids defaulted from the module name, and `mix chronicle.discover`. (#147)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Standalone constraint modules with `use Chronicle.Events.Constraint`, registered through `constraints:` or discovery, plus `remove_constraint/2` with per-value removal properties, `:scope` and `:event_sequences` on `unique_event_type`, violation message callbacks, and `Chronicle.Events.ConstraintViolation.details/1`. (#143)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Projections: a one-line `children` form, `removed_with_join/2`, `clear_with/2`, a default `:on` for joins inside `children`, and compile-time errors for a root `join` without `:on`, a duplicate property target and an unknown field. (#127)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Read models: shared, self-reconnecting subscriptions with `Chronicle.ReadModels.subscribe/2` and `unsubscribe/1`, `:filter` and `:sort` on `observe/2`, `observe_stream/2`, and `Chronicle.Projections.query/2`, `preview/2` and `wait_for/3`. (#144)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): `ConcurrencyScope` pipe helpers (`with_sequence_number/2`, `with_event_types/2`, `with_event_stream_id/2` and more) and `at_tail/2`, and an opt-in client-wide `concurrency: [default: :optimistic, check_first_append: true]` strategy. (#134)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Prepared events with `EventLog.prepare/2` and `append_prepared/3`, `get_metadata_at/2`, and `initiator_type` and `resolved_caused_by` in event metadata. (#150)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): `generation_for:` on `use Chronicle.Events.EventType` declares a generation of another event type, and the `strict_validation: true` client option rejects duplicate event type generations and migrators at startup. (#146)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Pinned generation delivery for reactors and reducers with `generation_delivery: :pinned`, or client-wide with `event_generation_delivery:`. (#119)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): `Chronicle.Patterns` queries behavior patterns, including `get_patterns_at/3` for a moment and `time_bucket/1`, which uses the same rule the engine mines with. (#39)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): OpenTelemetry spans and `:telemetry` events for appends, reactors and reducers, and W3C trace context on every gRPC call, following the shared Cratis convention. The package now depends on `opentelemetry_api`; spans are only exported when your application installs an OpenTelemetry SDK. See [Observability](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/blob/main/Documentation/observability.md). (#96)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.170.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.170.0)): `PLAY0690` warns when a reducer-backed read model cannot be keyed the way it is queried: a single-instance query looks the view up `by` a type that is not the event-source identity type of the events the reducer observes, or the reducer observes events whose producers have different identity types. Reducer instances are keyed by each event's event-source id, so the warning points to a projection with property keys, or a join, instead (part of #132)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.170.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.170.0)): The warning compares declared identity types only where every producer is known (commands with `for`, implicit or generated identifiers, and reactions with an explicit `for`), applies the reducer's `from` filters first, and stays silent for events from reactions without `for`, captures, seeds, code or other applications; `screenplay check`, MCP diagnostics and the editor host report it, and `--warnaserror` turns it into an error (part of #132)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.168.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.168.0)): `screenplay_declaration_details` describes a generated concept's rules: `generation.derivedPattern` is the anchored regular expression the strategy provably produces (or null with `derivedPatternUnknownReason`), and `generation.rules` lists every validation rule in authored order with its proof status (`proven`, `refuted` with a witness value, or `unproven` with a reason), plus `diagnosticCode` and `repair` when the compiler reported PLAY0688 or PLAY0689 at that rule. The proofs come from the same rule-consistency check the compiler uses. See [declaration details](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/mcp/reference.md). (part of #618)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.167.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.167.0)): The canonical corpus publishes the reference clock's schedule behavior as a versioned JSON fixture, `clock-schedules.v1.json`: the rules for epoch-counted intervals and daily, weekly and monthly UTC schedules, and boundary vectors with their expected due instants, including starts before the epoch, offset normalization, months without a 31st, 29 February in leap and non-leap years, the last calendar day and the occurrence limit (#754)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.167.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.167.0)): `ClockScheduleVectors` in `Cratis.Screenplay.CanonicalCorpus` exposes the fixture bytes with its version and SHA-256, so a consumer can pin it by hash; the file can also be fetched from the repository at the release tag (#754)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.98.0)): The Screenplay generator states a command specification asserting `result.ShouldNotBeAuthorized()` as `then denied` when the denial is explained by the stated authorization: the command has an emitted `authorize` gate with exactly recovered roles or policies, the scenario has an exactly recovered `given caller`, the pinned Screenplay reference runner denies that caller, and no other outcome is asserted. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3185)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.165.0)): A repository can declare its Screenplay validation gate in a committed `screenplay.json` at the model root: the `checks`, `warningsAsErrors`, `scope` and `openQuestions` the `screenplay` check command runs, so scripts and CI no longer repeat the options. The model root is the nearest folder at or above the target that holds `screenplay.json` or `.screenplay/`. A command-line option overrides only its own value, `--no-gate-file` ignores the file for one run, and a file with an unknown key or check name is refused with its location rather than ignored. See [decision 0076](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0076-declare-the-validation-gate-in-a-model-root-file.md) and the [`screenplay-v1` JSON schema](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/screenplay-v1.schema.json). (#739)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.165.0)): The `screenplay` check command gains `--format json`, which writes one JSON report with the diagnostics, open questions and the effective gate, and `--print-gate`, which prints the effective gate and where each value came from without compiling. (#739)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.165.0)): `ScreenplayValidationGate.Read`, `ValidationGateRoot.Resolve` and `ValidationGateResolver.Resolve` in `Cratis.Screenplay` let other tools read and apply the same gate. (#739)
- **cli** ([Release v3.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.63.0)): `cratis render` accepts Screenplay ESM v17 models: `then no events` renders for commands and single production-only reactions, and `when redelivered <Event> to <Reaction>` renders for a single named reaction trigger with event productions or one supported command invocation. Fan-out, deeper cascades and other shapes Stage cannot render yet are refused with `STAGE-ESM-011`. (#365)
- **cli** ([Release v3.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.63.0)): `cratis render` accepts Screenplay ESM v19 models: reaction invocations can declare `on refused`, `on refused by validation`, `on refused by constraint [Name]` and `on refused by authorization` branches that `produce` events from `$refusal.reason`, `$refusal.constraint` and `$refusal.message` or `acknowledge` the refusal. Shapes Stage cannot reproduce exactly are refused with `STAGE-ESM-039`. Models newer than v19 are refused with `CLI-RENDER-004`. (#365)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.97.0)): The Screenplay generator extracts reactors that handle command refusals with `Invoke.Command(...).OnRefused(...)` and `Acknowledge(...)` into `invokes … on refused [by validation | authorization | constraint …]` branches. Branches appear in declaration order. Branches that produce events map `CommandRefusal.Message` to `$refusal.message`, and the constraint name to `$refusal.constraint` under constraint selectors. A reactor whose callbacks cannot be proven pure, or whose output is capped below ESM v19, keeps its `file` reference and reports `SP0071`. (#3094)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.97.0)): New analyzer `ARCCHR0027` warns when an `OnRefused` branch returns something the runtime rejects, such as a command, a non-event object or an empty collection. See [ARCCHR0027](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/code-analysis/ARCCHR0027.md). (#3094)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.164.0)): Concepts that declare `generate` can carry validation rules: the compiler proves each rule against the generation strategy, binds a proven rule silently, reports a rule the strategy can break as `PLAY0688` with an example value that breaks it, and reports a rule it cannot decide as a `PLAY0689` warning with the reason (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.164.0)): Length, `not empty`, `equal`, `not equal` and `matches` rules are decided exactly for the regular-expression subset described in the generated-values documentation; `matches` is proven only for patterns a backtracking regex engine matches without ambiguity, so a proven rule never times out on a generated value (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.164.0)): Generated and specification fixtures are checked against their concept's rules as well as the strategy's shape; a fixture that breaks a rule is a contract violation (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.164.0)): A repair replaces a refuted rule with the `matches` pattern derived from the strategy (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.164.0)): Executable model version 20 carries rules on generated concepts; models without them keep their version, bytes and outcomes (part of #616)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.65.0)): `RegistrationOutcome.IsRegistering` says that the .NET client is registering its artifacts again, after a reconnect or a manual `RegisterAll`. The other members still describe the previous run, so `HasRun` and `IsSuccess` keep their meaning. (#4875)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.163.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.163.0)): `ScreenplayWorkspace.Rehome(documents, RehomeScope.Subset)` re-homes a workspace's identities onto a document set that holds only part of its model, such as the features a host has generated so far. Every declaration the documents contain keeps its semantic ID and its event contract ID, revision and origin; assignments for left-out declarations are dropped, so `ScreenplayModelRoot.Open` and `screenplay baseline admit`/`check` accept the state. Declarations the workspace does not know and documents that do not compile on their own are still refused. `Rehome(documents)` keeps its behaviour. See [Call these from your own tooling](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/tool.md#call-these-from-your-own-tooling). (#749)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.103.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.103.0)): Rendered specifications now cover `when redelivered <Event> to <Reaction>` for a single named reaction trigger with productions, or with one supported command invocation. The generated scenario records the givens with Chronicle's `History` and redelivers the located occurrence with its original sequence number, correlation and stream routing, so the input is never appended again. Fan-out, deeper cascades, constraints, read-model or query expectations, and redelivery combined with refusal branches are still refused with `STAGE-ESM-011`, which names the reason. (#371)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.96.0)): The Screenplay generator reads the application's react-router route table (`<Routes>`/`<Route path element>` declarations, or `createBrowserRouter`, `createHashRouter` and `createMemoryRouter` calls with literal route objects) and states navigation between screens: a menu item's `navigate('/literal')` becomes `item <name> navigate to <Screen>`, and a `CommandDialog` `onSuccess` navigation becomes the action's `navigate to <Screen>`. Required route parameters such as `:id` are bound with `parameter id from literal "42"`, and a screen in another slice is referenced by its qualified name. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3203)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.96.0)): Navigation is stated only when the destination is proven: the route table is the application's root table (it renders or creates the router, or it is the only file declaring routes), and exactly one known route can match the literal path. Ambiguous, computed, lazy, wildcard and nested route tables, `<Link to>`, relative navigation and navigation with options stay reported with SP0028. (#3203)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.102.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.102.0)): Stage accepts Screenplay ESM v19 models. A reaction's invocation can declare ordered `on refused` branches (any, validation, constraint, a named constraint, or authorization) that acknowledge the refusal or produce events from `$refusal.reason`, `$refusal.constraint` and `$refusal.message`. Rendered reactors use Arc's `OnRefused`/`Acknowledge`. Stage's semantic runs and the Stage Host settle them as Screenplay does: the first matching branch handles the refusal and stops the rest of that trigger, and a bare `on refused` never catches authorization. (#214)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.102.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.102.0)): Refusal branches Stage can't reproduce exactly fail with `STAGE-ESM-039`, which names the reason. That covers several refusing triggers on the same event, `$refusal.message` from a command with localized messages, and `$refusal.constraint` when several constraints can fail together. (#214)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.162.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.162.0)): `ScreenplayWorkspace.Rehome` re-homes a workspace's identities onto a different document split of the same model, so a host that writes its model to a repository in its own folder layout can produce a `.screenplay/identities.json` that `ScreenplayModelRoot.Open` and `screenplay baseline admit`/`check` accept. Every semantic ID and event contract ID, revision and origin is kept; re-homing refuses added or removed declarations, source that does not compile, and any document set a model root could not hold and discover again. See [Call these from your own tooling](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/tool.md#call-these-from-your-own-tooling). (#747)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.162.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.162.0)): `ModelRootDocumentKeys.For` returns the stable document key model-root discovery assigns to a path. (#747)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.161.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.161.0)): `then error` after `when query` (or after `then query … arguments` in a specification with no action) asserts that the query was rejected with a validation error, for example a lookup by an invoice number its concept's rules refuse. The form is admitted in executable semantic model version 18, and only models that use it select that version. (#682)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.95.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.95.0)): When a command appends to an event source whose id was generated by a `[GeneratedValue]` parameter in the same invocation, the append requires the whole event source to be empty. If it already has events, the command fails with `ConcurrencyViolation` and `ReasonDetail` `GeneratedIdentifierCollision`, and `Handle()` is not run again. This covers returned events (including `EventForEventSourceId`, `(id, event)` tuples and `EventsWithConcurrencyScopes`) and aggregates. See [Fresh identifiers](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/commands/fresh-identifiers.md). (#3129)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.95.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.95.0)): `[GeneratedValue(Freshness = GeneratedValueFreshness.Unguarded)]` opts a generated value out of the guard. (#3129)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.64.0)): The .NET client declares the global seeds it discovers from `ICanSeedEvents` as its program's authoritative set, keyed by `ChronicleOptions.ProgramIdentifier`, or by the entry assembly name when that is not set. Removing a seeder, or moving its seeds into `ForNamespace(...)`, therefore stops them reaching namespaces created afterwards. Events already appended stay where they are. See [seeding with C#](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/event-seeding/seeding-with-csharp.mdx) (#4869)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.94.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.94.0)): The Screenplay generator now writes a screen's `action`, toolbar `item … action` and `data … via query` references to commands and queries of another slice, qualified with the shortest slice scope that names exactly one declaration (for example `action Authors.Registration.RegisterAuthor`). The owning slice is found through the component's import. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3204)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.93.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.93.0)): Analyzer ARCCHR0023 reports a read model parameter that Arc cannot match to a single command property by its key type (#3251)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.93.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.93.0)): Analyzer ARCCHR0025 reports a command with no event source key that injects a read model. The read model is looked up by a newly generated id on every call and is never found (#3251)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.93.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.93.0)): Analyzer ARCCHR0026 reports a command or command validator that reads a Chronicle-backed read model through `IMongoCollection<T>` (#3251)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.63.0)): `[ReactorSideEffect]` marks a type as a reactor side-effect return type, so analyzer CHR0054 accepts reactor handlers that return it synchronously, or return an array or `IEnumerable<T>` of it. Hosts that register an `IReactorSideEffectHandler` for their own return types, such as Arc's command invocations, put it on those types. The marker only informs the analyzer; a registered handler still processes the value. See [CHR0054](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/code-analysis/CHR0054.mdx) (#4882)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): Stage accepts Screenplay ESM v17 models. Stage's semantic specification runs execute `when redelivered <Event> to <Reaction>`: the given event is delivered again to the named reaction only, without being appended again, and what follows is settled. They also execute `then no events` for commands, reactions and appends. (part of #371)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): Rendered command specifications and single production-only reaction specifications with `then no events` assert that nothing was appended or produced. Rendered redelivery, fan-out, deeper cascades and appends with no observer are refused with `STAGE-ESM-011`, which names the reason; rendering redelivery needs https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/issues/4873. (part of #371)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.92.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.92.0)): `IAsyncCommandExecutionScope`: a command execution scope can now await work in `BeginAsync`. It runs after the command context is built and before filters and validators. Validate-only calls do not run execution scopes (#3257)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.92.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.92.0)): `CommandScenario` can simulate a competing append with `AppendConcurrently` in commands that do not use decision reads (#3257)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.100.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.100.0)): Unique constraints now render on generated text values (`uuid`, `uuidv7`, `ulid`, `typeid`, `nanoid` and `bytes` concepts backed by String). A collision rejects the command with the named constraint violation, as in Screenplay. Each new attempt generates a fresh value; nothing is retried automatically. Specifications that assert a collision pin the generated values involved. (#377)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.2)): Observers report when their position moves past a live event with no evidence that it was delivered: the `chronicle-observer-events-suspected-skipped` counter and a rate-limited warning log name the observer, partition, sequence number and reason. Failed queue dispatches to an observer are counted by `chronicle-appended-events-queue-events-possibly-undelivered`. Delivery behaviour is unchanged; the troubleshooting guide explains how to investigate and recover a suspected skip (#4820)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.0)): Units of work accept enrollment contributors: `IUnitOfWork.AddEnrollmentContributor(IUnitOfWorkEnrollmentContributor)` returns a handle that removes the contributor when disposed. A contributor states the concurrency scope an event source must carry before any event for it is staged, through `AddEvent`, `AddEventWithNamedTags`, `AddEvents` and every `eventLog.Transactional` append. Equal scopes merge; a conflicting scope is rejected with `ConflictingEnrollmentScope` and nothing from that call is staged. This lets a host such as Arc guard appends that user code makes directly on the unit of work. See [Transactions](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/events/transactions.mdx). (#4861)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.2)): A guide, [Generated values](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/guides/generated-values.md), that follows a small shop model from `.play` to rendered code and runtime. It covers choosing a strategy and when not to generate a value, and every snippet comes from Stage's real output. The generated-values rendering reference links to it. (part of #175)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): Reaction refusal branches are executable: `invokes <Command>` can carry ordered `on refused [by validation | by constraint [Name] | by authorization]` branches that `produces` events or `acknowledge`; the first matching branch wins, a bare `on refused` covers validation and constraint refusals but never authorization, and a handled refusal stops the trigger's remaining invocations while other reactions continue (#433)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): `$refusal.reason`, `$refusal.constraint` and `$refusal.message` can be mapped in branch productions: the refusal kind, the first violated constraint's name, and the error messages joined with a newline exactly as authored (#433)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): Executable model version 19 carries refusal branches as `onRefused` on invocations and refusal values as `refusal` expressions, written only when used; models without them keep their version, bytes and outcomes (#433)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): The Invoicing sample records a refused payment reconciliation as `PaymentReconciliationRefused` (#433)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.61.0)): `ReactorScenario` can redeliver a recorded occurrence with its original context. `Given.ForEventSource(id).History(...)` (also after `OnStream(...)`) records events without invoking the reactor and returns a `RecordedOccurrence` with the event and its `EventContext`; `scenario.Redeliver(occurrence)` delivers it again with the same sequence number, correlation, occurred time and stream routing, so a `ReactorDelivery` parameter sees the same identity as for the original delivery. Observer filters, read-model seeding and produced-event recording behave as for `Events(...)`, for class-based and fluent reactor scenarios. See [Reactor scenarios](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/testing/reactors/scenario.mdx). (#4873)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.60.0)): Webhooks can opt in to receive every event in the generation they subscribed to, on live delivery, catch-up, replay and partition retries alike. Opt in with `WithGenerationDelivery(EventGenerationDelivery.Pinned)` on the .NET webhook builder, or set `GenerationDelivery` to `Pinned` when registering webhooks directly. The kernel records each subscribed event type's generation with the webhook and selects, decrypts and, when needed, migrates the event to that generation before posting it; the payload envelope is unchanged. Webhooks that do not opt in are unchanged. See [webhooks](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/webhooks/index.mdx) (part of #4710)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.159.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.159.0)): `ScreenplayModelRoot.Open` and `ScreenplayModelRoot.OpenForReleaseBaselineCheck` in `Cratis.Screenplay` open a model root read-only with its persisted identities and release baseline, or return a typed failure such as `PendingOperation`, `PersistedIdentitiesRequired`, `IdentityStateConflict`, `MetadataPermissions` or `UnreadableModelRoot`. Renderers and tools can now assess a release baseline on exactly the model they compile, through the same code the MCP server and `screenplay baseline check` use. See [Call these from your own tooling](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/tool.md#call-these-from-your-own-tooling). (#740) (#741)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.159.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.159.0)): `PersistedIdentityStateSerializer` reads and writes `.screenplay/identities.json`, so consumers no longer need to copy its format. (#741)
- **cli** ([Release v3.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.61.0)): `cratis render` (publish, `--check` and `--scope`) reports Stage's release-baseline warnings when a render would change (`STAGE-BASELINE-001`) or drop (`STAGE-BASELINE-002`) an event revision admitted to the model's release baseline, or when the baseline cannot be read (`STAGE-BASELINE-003`) or checked (`STAGE-BASELINE-004`). The warnings cover the whole application, also for scoped renders, and never change the exit code, the written artifacts or the receipt; keep `cratis screenplay baseline check` as the gate in CI. See [Release-baseline warnings](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/reference/screenplay.md#release-baseline-warnings). (#356)
- **cli** ([Release v3.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.61.0)): `cratis render --workspace <FILE> --baseline <FILE>` selects the release baseline for workspace input; without it, render uses `.screenplay/release-baseline.json` beside the workspace file. PATH input reads the baseline from the model root's `.screenplay/` folder. (#356)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.0)): A conditional production renders as a guarded event in the generated command handler. The condition is evaluated over the command's values and generated values, and productions whose condition does not hold are skipped. When every production is skipped, the command is accepted without appending. (#360)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.0)): Freshness guards on generated event sources (ESM v15) apply only to productions that are taken, so a skipped production never causes a false concurrency rejection. (#360)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.0)): The Stage Host and the spec runner execute only taken productions, matching Screenplay's reference evaluation. (#360)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.0)): A state-change slice may declare several commands; each renders with its own record, validator and response, alongside the slice's shared events and read models. (#360)
- **cli** ([Release v3.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.60.0)): `cratis screenplay baseline check [ROOT]` and `cratis screenplay baseline admit [ROOT] --release <LABEL> [--from-esm <FILE>]` check a model against its committed release baseline and admit a release into it, using Screenplay's own baseline rules. They exit 0 when clean or admitted, 1 on violations and 2 when they could not run, so a CI job can gate on `check` and a release job can run `admit --release $TAG`. See the [Screenplay command reference](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/reference/screenplay.md). (#351)
- **cli** ([Release v3.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.60.0)): `cratis screenplay validate --open-questions report|fail` reads the model's open questions from its notes. `report` prints them and keeps the validation result; `fail` exits 5 while an open question remains, and unreadable notes exit 1. (Cratis/Screenplay#694)
- **cli** ([Release v3.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.60.0)): `cratis render` admits ESM v16: an event's `id` pin keeps its stored event type name when you rename the event. (#353)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.90.0)): Arc.Chronicle recognizes Chronicle self-reducing read models, so injecting one into a command, validator or `Provide()` reads it through Chronicle, including personal-data release, instead of falling back to reading MongoDB directly. Requires Chronicle 19.58.0 or later in the application. (#3233)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.90.0)): Screenplay recognizes self-reducing read models as reducer-built, including reducers whose event parameter is an interface or base class, and reports when it cannot list every observed event. (#3233)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.97.0)): `StageReleaseBaseline.Check` builds a `ReleaseBaselineAssessment` from a Screenplay workspace or a compilation and a release baseline, using Screenplay's own baseline comparison. `SemanticModelLoader` reads `.screenplay/release-baseline.json` from the model root (it never writes it) and exposes the result as `ReleaseBaseline`. Callers that already hold an assessment pass it on `ArtifactRenderRequest.ReleaseBaseline` or through `PlanFrom`. (#375)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.97.0)): Render plans that carry an assessment report warnings for the whole application, whatever the render scope: `STAGE-BASELINE-001` when an admitted event revision changed, `STAGE-BASELINE-002` when it is missing, `STAGE-BASELINE-003` when the baseline cannot be read or belongs to another application, and `STAGE-BASELINE-004` when the check could not run or was made for a different model revision. A plan with only these warnings still succeeds with the same artifacts. Without an assessment, plans are unchanged. (#375)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.97.0)): The rendering guides explain where the baseline comes from, how these warnings relate to the `screenplay baseline check` gate and to the publication check, and that callers that pass only an executable model get no baseline warnings. (#375)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.157.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.157.0)): `then no events` is executable: it asserts that the action produced no new facts, including everything its reactions appended, and after `when append` it asserts that no reaction appended anything (part of #433)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.157.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.157.0)): `when redelivered <Event> to <Reaction>` is executable: the located given event is delivered again to the named reaction only, without appending it again, and the ordinary cascade settles; an unhandled refused invocation stays assertable with `then error` (part of #433)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.157.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.157.0)): Executable model version 17 carries both forms as `thenNoEvents` and `whenRedelivered` members, written only when used; models without them keep their version, bytes and outcomes (part of #433)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.89.0)): The Screenplay generator recovers event source and stream model names from Stage-rendered `{Model}EventSource` classes and from directly referenced stream constants, and states a differing stored name with `id`. (#3218)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.89.0)): Reactions that append through an explicit `EventForEventSourceId` destination, including list accumulator bodies, become reaction productions with their route when the route is provable. (#3218)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): Events with a stored name that differs from their model name (`id "Original"` in the model, carried as `storedName` in ESM v16) render as `[EventType("Original")]`, while the generated class follows the current name. Unpinned events and models below v16 render exactly as before. The Host registers, appends and rebuilds these events under the stored name. (#372)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): `CratisRendering.CheckPublication` refuses a plan that would change the Chronicle `[EventType]` id of an existing event class in the destination, or whose existing id cannot be read. The new outcome is `CratisPublicationCheck.ChangesEventTypeIds`, which lists each change and reports `STAGE-EVENT-002`. Publishers that switch over `CratisPublicationCheck` must handle this new case. For scoped plans the aggregate policy layout check still runs first, so publishers that refuse only `RequiresApplicationScope` keep refusing those plans. `cratis render` adopts the new refusal in Cratis/cli#354. (#372)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): A plan where two events would get the same Chronicle event type id, for example a pinned `id "RecordedEvent"` beside an unpinned `Recorded_event`, fails with `STAGE-ESM-012`. (#372)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): Protected observable list queries now render their success specifications with Arc's `QueryScenario` and its default Chronicle read models. Scoped projections and protected snapshot list successes are still refused with `STAGE-ESM-011`. (part of #278)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.59.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.59.4)): `Cratis:Chronicle:Shutdown:DrainTimeout` (environment variable `Cratis__Chronicle__Shutdown__DrainTimeout`) (default 8 seconds) bounds how long the kernel spends finishing in-flight observer work when it stops; the effective budget is capped below the host shutdown timeout and logged at startup (#4790)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.95.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.95.0)): Command specifications with a `given clock` now render and run instead of being refused as unsupported time. The rendered specification fixes Arc's scenario clock to the stated instant, and Stage's semantic specification runs use it as the command's occurrence. So `$context.occurred` values can be asserted exactly, and generated time-ordered values are checked against that instant. The clock must be a whole number of milliseconds and no earlier than 1970. `when clock`, clocks on reaction specifications, and query-only and direct-append specifications are still refused. (#381)
